and (i)
d_{i}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}b_{i}
) and
d_{i}^{\prime}
is wgp,
and hence
b_{i+1}d_{i}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{b_{i}}ad_{i}
(by
b_{i+1}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(d_{i}^{\prime}b_{i})
and
a\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(b_{i}d_{i})
).

Claim 7.13
.

For all
i\in\omega
,

\dim^{0}(d_{i})\leq\dim^{0}(d_{i+1})\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(d_{i+1})\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)
.

Proof.

The first inequality is by (ii).

By (v),
d_{i+1}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\bar{h})
for some
\boldsymbol{\delta}
-independent sequence

\bar{h}

of realisations of
{\operatorname{tp}}(dd^{\prime})
. But
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}d^{\prime}
, so
\bar{h}
is a

\boldsymbol{\delta}
-independent sequence of realisations of the types
{\operatorname{tp}}(d)
and

{\operatorname{tp}}(d^{\prime})
, which are cgp. So Lemma
5.13
(ii) yields the second
inequality (since we assumed
\dim^{0}(d)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)
).

Finally, (i) and Lemma
7.7
give the third inequality.
∎
7.13

By Claim
7.13
,
\dim^{0}(d_{i+1})=\dim^{0}(d_{i})
for some
i\in\omega
.
Then by
d_{i}\sim^{0}_{d_{i+1}}d_{i}^{\prime}
, we have
d_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}d_{i+1}
(since

\dim^{0}(d_{i})=\dim^{0}(d_{i+1})\geq\dim^{0}(d_{i+1}/d_{i})\geq\dim^{0}(d_{i}^{\prime}/d_{i})=\dim^{0}(d_{i}^{\prime})=\dim^{0}(d_{i})
(using
d_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}d_{i}^{\prime}
)), so

\dim^{0}(d_{i+1})=\dim^{0}(d_{i+1}/d_{i})
).

Hence, as illustrated in the picture below, Proposition
6.4
applies to
(d_{i},a,b_{i},d_{i}^{\prime},b_{i+1})

and
d_{i+1}={\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(ab_{i+1}/d_{i}d_{i}^{\prime})
(conditions (a)-(d) have been shown
for all
i
, while (e) is the condition
d_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}d_{i+1}
just shown for
our choice of
i
), so
(d_{i},d_{i}^{\prime},d_{i+1},a,b_{i},b_{i+1})
is a strict
algebraic quadrangle yielding a CFAHS
(G,X)
defined over

{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
.

\textstyle{d_{i+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{d_{i}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{d_{i}}
\textstyle{b_{i+1}}
\textstyle{a}
\textstyle{b_{i}\;\;\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Since
{\operatorname{tp}}(a)
is cgp and
{\operatorname{tp}}(d_{i})
is wgp,
G
is commutative by
Theorem
5.18
, and up to interalgebraicity,
(a,d_{i},b_{i})
is
generic in the graph of addition of
G
.
So
(a,d_{i},b_{i})
is abelian.

It follows that
d_{i}\sim^{0}_{b_{i}}a
, hence
\dim^{0}(d_{i})=\dim^{0}(d_{i}/b_{i})=\dim^{0}(a/b_{i})=\dim^{0}(a)
. But
\dim^{0}(d_{0})=\dim^{0}(d_{0}/b_{0})\geq\dim^{0}(a/b_{0})=\dim^{0}(a)
, and so by (ii) we conclude that already

\dim^{0}(d_{1})=\dim^{0}(d_{0})
. In other words, we may actually take
i=0
in the
above argument to show that
(d_{0},d_{0}^{\prime},d_{1},a,b_{0},b_{1})=(d,d^{\prime},e,a,b,a^{\prime})
is a strict
algebraic quadrangle and
(a,d,b)
is abelian.
∎

7.3.
Abelianity of semi-cgp correspondence triangles

We now proceed to the proof of our main theorem, Theorem
7.20
, which
improves on Proposition
7.12
by proving abelianity of a semi-cgp
correspondence triangle
(a,d,b)
without assuming that
d
is cgp. In
outline, this will go as follows. We find
c
such that
{\operatorname{tp}}(d/c)
is cgp and
apply Proposition
7.12
over
c
, yielding a family of groups

(G_{c})_{c}
. Using the “moreover” clause in Proposition
7.12
, we find
that these groups are pairwise isogenous. Composing isogenies, we see that
d

can be identified with an element of an algebraic group of affine linear
transformations of such a group, allowing us to conclude via
Corollary
5.22
.

We begin with some preliminary definitions and results.

Definition 7.14
.

If
G
is a commutative algebraic group over
C\subseteq K
and
x,y\in G(K)
, we write
x\approx_{C}y
to mean that
y-x\in G({\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(C))
.
We abbreviate
x\approx_{\emptyset}y
to
x\approx y
.

Fact 7.15
.

Suppose
G
and
H
are connected commutative algebraic groups over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
,
and
(x,a)\in G^{2}
and
(x^{\prime},a^{\prime})\in H^{2}
are generic,
and
x\sim^{0}x^{\prime}
and
a\sim^{0}a^{\prime}
and
x+a\sim^{0}x^{\prime}+a^{\prime}
.
Then there exist
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
and an isogeny
\alpha:G\to H
over

{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
such that
\alpha x\approx nx^{\prime}
.

Proof.

This is essentially the “moreover” clause of
[
1
, Fact 2.13]
;
that
\alpha
is over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
was not specified there, but this
follows from the proof, since the graph of
\alpha
is obtained from the
stabiliser of
{\operatorname{tp}}^{0}(xx^{\prime}/{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset))
(as explained in

[
4
, Lemme 2.4]
).
∎

Fact 7.16
.

Let
G
be a commutative algebraic group over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
. Denote by
{\operatorname{Aut}}(G)
the group of algebraic group automorphisms of
G
.
Then there exists a connected algebraic group
{\operatorname{Aut}}^{0}(G)\leq{\operatorname{Aut}}(G)
over

{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
such that any
\sigma\in{\operatorname{Aut}}(G)
is of the form
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}\circ\sigma_{0}
where
\sigma_{0}\in{\operatorname{Aut}}(G)
is defined over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
and
\sigma^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{Aut}}^{0}(G)
.

Proof.

We first recall from
[
2
, Lemma 3.1]
the following facts about the
structure of commutative algebraic groups in characteristic 0.

•

There exists an exact sequence
0\to V\to G\to S\to 0

where
V
is a vector group and
S
is a semiabelian variety,
all over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
.

•

G=G_{0}\oplus V_{0}
where
G_{0}:=G[\infty]^{\operatorname{Zar}}

is the Zariski closure of the torsion
and
V_{0}\leq V
is a vector subgroup defined over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)

such that
V=(V\cap G_{0})\oplus V_{0}
.

Let
{\operatorname{Aut}}^{0}(G)
be the kernel of the restriction homomorphism
\restriction_{G_{0}}:{\operatorname{Aut}}(G)\to{\operatorname{Aut}}(G_{0})
.
If
\sigma_{0}\in{\operatorname{Aut}}(G_{0})
then
\sigma_{0}
is determined by its action on

G[\infty]\subseteq{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
by the density and the fact that
\sigma_{0}
is an algebraic automorphism, so
\sigma_{0}
is over

{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
.
Now if
\sigma\in{\operatorname{Aut}}(G)
, then
\sigma\negmedspace\restriction_{G_{0}}
extends uniquely to
\sigma_{0}\in{\operatorname{Aut}}(G)
over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
with
\sigma_{0}\negmedspace\restriction_{V_{0}}=\operatorname{id}_{V_{0}}
, and then
\sigma^{\prime}:=\sigma\circ\sigma_{0}^{-1}\in{\operatorname{Aut}}^{0}(G)
as required.
So it remains only to see that
{\operatorname{Aut}}^{0}(G)
is a
connected algebraic group.

Let
L\leq{\operatorname{GL}}(V)
be the stabiliser of
V\cap G_{0}
in
{\operatorname{GL}}(V)
.
Now
V
is the (unique) maximal vector subgroup of
G
, since no non-trivial
homomorphism
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\to S
exists, so any element of
{\operatorname{Aut}}^{0}(G)
restricts to
an element of
L
.
This yields an isomorphism
\restriction_{V}:{\operatorname{Aut}}^{0}(G)\to L
, since
any
\theta\in L
extends uniquely to
\sigma\in{\operatorname{Aut}}^{0}(G)
defined on
G=G_{0}\oplus V_{0}
by
\sigma(g_{0}+v_{0}):=g_{0}+\theta(v_{0})
.
Finally,
L
is a connected algebraic group over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
, as required.
∎

Lemma 7.17
.

If
(a,d,b)
is a correspondence triangle, and
f\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}adb
and
(a,d,b)

is abelian over
f
, then
(a,d,b)
is abelian.

Proof.

{\operatorname{tp}}^{0}(f/adb)
is then finitely satisfiable in
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
, and so
the existence of
G
over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f)
of a certain dimension and
g_{a}\sim^{0}_{f}a
,
g_{d}\sim^{0}_{f}d
, and
g_{b}\sim^{0}_{f}b
with
g_{b}=g_{a}+g_{d}

implies the corresponding existence over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
, and the
genericity follows since
\dim^{0}(a)=\dim^{0}(a/f)
etc.
∎

Given
a
,
B
, and
C
, we denote by
{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}_{C}(a/B)
the value of
{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(a/B)

in the language
{\mathcal{L}}(C)
obtained by adding constants for
C
.

Lemma 7.18
.

For all
a
,
B
, and
C
, we have
{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}_{C}(a/B)\sim^{0}_{C}{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(a/BC)
.

Proof.

We use the description above of
{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}
in terms of fields of definition (see the discussion before Fact
5.2
).
Let
E_{0}
be the constants of
{\mathcal{L}}
(which form a field by assumption).
Let
k
be the field of definition of
{\operatorname{loc}}^{0}(a/{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(BC))={\operatorname{loc}}^{0}(a/{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}_{C}(B))
.
Then
E_{0}({\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(a/BC))=E_{0}(k)

and
E_{0}(C,{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}_{C}(a/B))=E_{0}(C,k)
,
so
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(C,{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}_{C}(a/B))={\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(C,k)={\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(C,{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(a/BC))
, as
required.

Alternatively, in stability theoretic terms:
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}_{C}({\operatorname{cb}}^{0}_{C}(a/B))
is the
smallest
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}_{C}
-closed set over which
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(BC))
doesn’t fork,
and so is
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(C,{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(a/BC))
.
∎

Lemma 7.19
.

If
(a,d,b)
is a correspondence triangle over
C={\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(C)
, possibly semi-cgp or cgp, then
(a,d,b)
is a
correspondence triangle (respectively semi-cgp or cgp) in the language
{\mathcal{L}}(C)
obtained by adding
constants for
C
.

Proof.

The only property which is not quite immediate is that
d\sim^{0}_{C}{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}_{C}(ab/d)
, but by Lemma
7.18
this follows from
d\sim^{0}_{C}{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(ab/Cd)
.
∎

Theorem 7.20
.

Every semi-cgp correspondence triangle is abelian.

Proof.

Let
(a,d,b)
be a semi-cgp correspondence triangle.

If
{\operatorname{tp}}(d)
is cgp, we are done by Proposition
7.12
. So suppose not.

By Lemma
5.8
(ii),
let
c
be such that
{\operatorname{tp}}(d/c)
is cgp and broad and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(c)<\infty
.
Suppose for a contradiction that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(c)=0
. Then
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}c
since
{\operatorname{tp}}(d)
is wgp, and then if
e
is such that
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}e
, then also
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{c}e
, so
\boldsymbol{\delta}(d/ec)=0
, hence also
\boldsymbol{\delta}(d/e)=0
. This contradicts
{\operatorname{tp}}(d)
not being cgp.
So
{\operatorname{tp}}(c)
is broad.
We may assume
c\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{d}ab
.

By Lemma
5.8
(i), there exists
f
with
\boldsymbol{\delta}(f)=0
such
that
{\operatorname{tp}}(c/f)
is wgp. Then
f\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}ad
and
f\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}adb
since
{\operatorname{tp}}(ad)
is wgp by Remark
5.10
. Adding
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f)
as constants to the language
preserves the assumptions that
(a,d,b)
is a semi-cgp correspondence triangle and that
{\operatorname{tp}}(d/c)
is cgp. Indeed, since
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{c}f
and
{\operatorname{tp}}(d/c)
is broad and cgp, we get
{\operatorname{tp}}(d/cf)
is broad and cgp by Lemma
5.12
(ii) and independence.
Similarly,
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/f)
is cgp and
{\operatorname{tp}}(d/f)
is wgp (by Fact
5.9
(iii)).
Thus by Lemma
7.17
, we may assume that
{\operatorname{tp}}(c)
is wgp.

By Lemma
7.8
,
(a,d,b)
is a cgp correspondence triangle over

c
. So by Proposition
7.12

applied via Lemma
7.19
in the expansion
{\mathcal{L}}({\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c))
,
there is a
connected commutative algebraic group
(G_{c},+)
over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c)
such that
d,a,b

are interalgebraic over
c
with generics

d_{c},a_{c},b_{c}\in G_{c}
respectively, and
b_{c}=a_{c}+d_{c}
.
In particular,
d\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(cab)
.

Let
G:=G_{c}
.

Claim 7.21
.

a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}dc
and
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}dc
.

Proof.

Since
c\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{d}a
and
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}a
, by transitivity, we have
cd\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}a
. Similarly, we get
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}dc
.
∎
7.21

Take
d^{\prime}a^{\prime}c^{\prime}
such that
d^{\prime}a^{\prime}c^{\prime}\equiv_{{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(b)}dac
and
d^{\prime}a^{\prime}c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{b}dac
.

Claim 7.22
.

a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}cc^{\prime}dd^{\prime}
,
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}cc^{\prime}dd^{\prime}
, and
a^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}cc^{\prime}dd^{\prime}
. Thus, since
{\operatorname{tp}}(a)
and
{\operatorname{tp}}(b)
are wgp (by Lemma
7.2
), the corresponding independences also hold for
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}
.

Proof.

By symmetry, it is enough to prove
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}cc^{\prime}dd^{\prime}
. We have
d^{\prime}c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{b}dac
, hence

(3)

d^{\prime}c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}dcab

by
d^{\prime}c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}b
(Claim
7.21
and
d^{\prime}c^{\prime}\equiv_{b}dc
). Thus,

d^{\prime}c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{dc}a

and since
dc\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}a
by Claim
7.21
, we conclude
dcd^{\prime}c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}a
.
∎
7.22

Let
e:={\operatorname{cb}}^{0}_{cc^{\prime}}(aa^{\prime}/dd^{\prime})
.

Claim 7.23
.

(d,d^{\prime},e,a,b,a^{\prime})
is a strict algebraic partial quadrangle over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c,c^{\prime})
, in particular
a\sim^{0}_{ecc^{\prime}}a^{\prime}
and
d\sim^{0}_{ecc^{\prime}}d^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We claim that the conditions for the moreover part in Proposition
7.12
hold for
(a,d,b)
and
(a^{\prime},d^{\prime},b)
when we pass to the expansion
{\mathcal{L}}({\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c,c^{\prime}))
. Since
(a,d,b)
is a cgp correspondence triangle over
c
and
c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{dc}ab
by (
3
), and
{\operatorname{tp}}(d/cc^{\prime})
is broad and cgp by
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{c}c^{\prime}
(which again follows from (
3
)) and Lemma
5.12
(ii), we conclude that
(a,d,b)
is a cgp correspondence triangle over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c,c^{\prime})
by Lemma
7.8
.

By symmetry,
(a^{\prime},d^{\prime},b)
is also a cgp correspondence triangle over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c,c^{\prime})
. Moreover,
\boldsymbol{\delta}(d/cc^{\prime})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(d/c)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(d/bc)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(d^{\prime}/bc^{\prime})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(d^{\prime}/c^{\prime})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(d^{\prime}/cc^{\prime})
by
dc\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}c^{\prime}
,
dc\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}b
and symmetrically
d^{\prime}c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}c
and
d^{\prime}c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}b
. Since
{\operatorname{tp}}(c),{\operatorname{tp}}(c^{\prime}),{\operatorname{tp}}(b)
are each wgp, an analogous calculation shows
\dim^{0}(d/cc^{\prime})=\dim^{0}(d^{\prime}/cc^{\prime})
. We only need to show

. By (
3
), we have
d^{\prime}c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}dcab
, hence
d^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{bcc^{\prime}}da
and
a^{\prime}d^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{bcc^{\prime}}da
as

. We conclude by applying Proposition
7.12
in
{\mathcal{L}}({\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c,c^{\prime}))
, via Lemma

.
∎
7.23

Claim 7.24
.

There exist a connected algebraic group
H
over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime})

and isomorphisms
\theta_{a},\theta_{b}:G\to H
over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(cc^{\prime})

and
a_{H},b_{H}\in H(K)

such that

\theta_{a}(a_{c})\approx_{cc^{\prime}}a_{H}\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}a\text{ and }\theta_{b}(b_{c})\approx_{cc^{\prime}}b_{H}\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}b.

Proof.

Take
a^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}},b_{c^{\prime}},d^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}
such that
a^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}b_{c^{\prime}}d^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}a^{\prime}d^{\prime}c^{\prime}\equiv_{{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(b)}a_{c}b_{c}d_{c}adc
, so
b_{c^{\prime}}=a^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}+d^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}
in

G_{c^{\prime}}
, a connected algebraic group over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime})
in which
b_{c^{\prime}}
is
generic over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime})
,
and
b_{c^{\prime}}\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}b
.

We have
a^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}a^{\prime}\sim^{0}_{ecc^{\prime}}a
by Claim
7.23
, and similarly,
d^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}d^{\prime}\sim^{0}_{ecc^{\prime}}d
.
Now
{\operatorname{tp}}(e/{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(abdcc^{\prime}))
is finitely satisfiable in
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(cc^{\prime})
,
since
e\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{cc^{\prime}}abd
by Claim
7.23
.
so there exist
a^{\prime}_{0}\sim^{0}_{cc^{\prime}}a
and
d^{\prime}_{0}\sim^{0}_{cc^{\prime}}d
such that

a^{\prime}_{0},d^{\prime}_{0}\in G_{c^{\prime}}
and
b_{c^{\prime}}=a^{\prime}_{0}+d^{\prime}_{0}
,
since this existence statement holds over
ecc^{\prime}
witnessed by
a^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}
and
d^{\prime}_{c^{\prime}}
.
By Fact
7.15
,

\alpha b_{c^{\prime}}\approx_{cc^{\prime}}nb_{c}
for some
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
and isogeny
\alpha:G_{c^{\prime}}\to G
over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(cc^{\prime})
. Let
H:=G_{c^{\prime}}/\ker\alpha
. Note that
H
is divisible since
G_{c^{\prime}}
is (by the fact that
G_{c^{\prime}}
is connected). Taking
b_{H}
to be an
n
-division point of
b_{c^{\prime}}/\ker\alpha
, we obtain
an isomorphism
\theta_{b}
as required,
noting that
H
is over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime})
since
\ker\alpha\leq G_{c^{\prime}}
is
finite and hence consists of finitely many torsion elements of
G_{c^{\prime}}
,
each of which is in
G_{c^{\prime}}({\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime}))
, since each
n
-torsion group
G_{c^{\prime}}[n]
is finite.

Now by symmetry of
a
and
b
, an identical argument yields that we may
find
c^{\prime\prime}\equiv_{{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(a)}c
with
c^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{a}c
,
and
H^{\prime\prime}
over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime\prime})
and an isomorphism
\theta_{a}^{\prime\prime}:G\to H^{\prime\prime}

over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(cc^{\prime\prime})
with
\theta_{a}^{\prime\prime}(a_{c})\approx_{cc^{\prime\prime}}a_{H^{\prime\prime}}\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime\prime}}a
.

Now
c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}c
, so
c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}c
since
{\operatorname{tp}}(c)
is wgp, and similarly

c^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}c
.
Also
c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}ac
(since
c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}ac
by (
3
) and
{\operatorname{tp}}(c^{\prime})
being wgp), and similarly
c^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}ac
. But
c^{\prime}\equiv_{{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)}c\equiv_{{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)}c^{\prime\prime}
,
so

c^{\prime}\equiv^{0}_{{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(ac)}c^{\prime\prime}
by stationarity of
{\operatorname{tp}}^{0}(c/{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset))
.

So we obtain
H^{\prime}
over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime})
and an isomorphism
\theta_{a}^{\prime}:G\to H^{\prime}
over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(cc^{\prime})
with
\theta_{a}^{\prime}(a_{c})\approx_{cc^{\prime}}a_{H^{\prime}}\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}a
.

Now
\theta_{b}\circ(\theta_{a}^{\prime})^{-1}
is an isomorphism
H^{\prime}\cong H
over

{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(cc^{\prime})
, so by elementarity of the field embedding
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime})\preceq{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(cc^{\prime})
there is an isomorphism
\phi:H^{\prime}\cong H
over

{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime})
.
Setting
\theta_{a}:=\phi\circ\theta_{a}^{\prime}:G\cong H
and

, we conclude.
∎
7.24

Now let
\sigma:=\theta_{b}\circ\theta_{a}^{-1}\in{\operatorname{Aut}}(H)
.
Then

\sigma a_{H}+\theta_{b}(d_{c})\approx_{cc^{\prime}}\theta_{b}(a_{c})+\theta_{b}(d_{c})=\theta_{b}(b_{c})\approx_{cc^{\prime}}b_{H},

so
h:=b_{H}-\sigma a_{H}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(dcc^{\prime})
.

By Fact
7.16
,

\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}\sigma_{0}
where
\sigma_{0}
is over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime})
and

\sigma^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{Aut}}^{0}(H)
. Let
a_{H}^{\prime}:=\sigma_{0}(a_{H})
, note that
a_{H}\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}a_{H}^{\prime}
.
Let
\alpha:=(h,\sigma^{\prime})\in H\rtimes{\operatorname{Aut}}^{0}(H)
.
Then
\alpha\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(dcc^{\prime})
and
\alpha*a_{H}^{\prime}=b_{H}
.
Since the action is faithful,
{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}_{c^{\prime}}(ab/\alpha)\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}\alpha
. We claim that
\alpha\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}d
. Note that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}dcc^{\prime}
and
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}dcc^{\prime}
(by Claim
7.22
), and
{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(ab/dcc^{\prime})\sim^{0}d
by
ab\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{d}cc^{\prime}
(as
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{d}cc^{\prime}
and
b\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(ad)
) and
{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(ab/d)\sim^{0}d
. Since
\alpha\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(dcc^{\prime})
and
a\sim^{0}_{\alpha c^{\prime}}b
, we get
d\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\alpha c^{\prime})
by Fact
5.2
. On the other hand
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{c^{\prime}}\alpha
(by
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}\alpha c^{\prime}
as
\alpha\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(dcc^{\prime})
),
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{c^{\prime}}\alpha
and
{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}_{c^{\prime}}(ab/\alpha)\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}\alpha
. Since
d\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\alpha c^{\prime})
and
a\sim^{0}_{dc^{\prime}}b
we have
\alpha\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(dc^{\prime})
as required.

Note that
a_{H}^{\prime}
is generic in
H
over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime})
, that
{\operatorname{tp}}(\alpha/{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime}))
is wgp because
{\operatorname{tp}}(d/{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime}))
is, that similarly
{\operatorname{tp}}(a_{H}^{\prime}/{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime}))
is cgp, and that
a_{H}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{c^{\prime}}\alpha\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{c^{\prime}}b_{H}
as
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{c^{\prime}}d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{c^{\prime}}b
and
a\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}a_{H}\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}a_{H}^{\prime}
,
d\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}\alpha
,
b_{H}\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}b
. By Corollary
5.22
,
\alpha
is generic in a left coset over

{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime})
of an abelian subgroup
G^{\prime}
of
H\rtimes{\operatorname{Aut}}^{0}(H)
,
so say
\alpha_{0}\in(H\rtimes{\operatorname{Aut}}^{0}(H))({\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c^{\prime}))
and

\alpha_{0}\cdot\alpha\in G^{\prime}
. Then
(\alpha_{0}\cdot\alpha)*a_{H}^{\prime}=\alpha_{0}*b_{H}
, and
a_{H}^{\prime}\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}a
and
\alpha_{0}\cdot\alpha\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}d
and
\alpha_{0}*b_{H}\sim^{0}_{c^{\prime}}b
,
and so the triangle
(a,b,d)
is abelian over
c^{\prime}
. Note that
c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}abd
by (
3
) and
{\operatorname{tp}}(abd)
being wgp.
We conclude by Lemma
7.17
.
∎

8.
Asymmetric Elekes-Szabó

In this section, we translate our main results from the pseudofinite setting to obtain elementary finitary statements generalising the Elekes-Szabó theorem.

First, we recall some definitions from
[
1
]
. Bear in mind our convention that “subvariety” means “closed subvariety”.

Definition 8.1
.

A
generically finite algebraic correspondence
between
irreducible algebraic varieties
V
and
V^{\prime}
is an irreducible
subvariety of the product
Z\subseteq V\times V^{\prime}
such that the
projections
\pi_{V}(Z)\subseteq V
and
\pi_{V^{\prime}}(Z)\subseteq V^{\prime}
are
Zariski dense, and
\dim(Z)=\dim(V)=\dim(V^{\prime})
.

Note that if
V
,
V^{\prime}
, and
Z
are defined over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
, the
condition on
Z
is equivalent to being of the form
Z={\operatorname{loc}}^{0}((v,v^{\prime}))
for
some generic
v\in V
and
v^{\prime}\in V^{\prime}
with
v\sim^{0}v^{\prime}
.

Definition 8.2
.

If
W_{1},W_{2},W_{3}
are irreducible algebraic varieties over an algebraically closed field,
we say an irreducible subvariety
U\subseteq W_{1}\times W_{2}\times W_{3}
is in
co-ordinatewise correspondence
with the graph
\Gamma_{+}=\{(x,y,z)\in G^{3}:x+y=z\}
of addition in a commutative algebraic group
G
if there exists a generically finite algebraic correspondence
Z\subseteq U\times\Gamma_{+}
such that the Zariski closures of the projections
(\pi_{i}\times\pi^{\prime}_{i})(Z)\subseteq W_{i}\times G
are generically finite algebraic correspondences between
W_{i}
and
G
for
i=1,2,3
. (Technically, this is a property of the tuple
(U,W_{1},W_{2},W_{3},G)
, so we always write explicitly
U\subseteq W_{1}\times W_{2}\times W_{3}
.)

If
U,W_{1},W_{2},W_{3},(G;+)
are defined over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
, this is equivalent to there existing generic elements
(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3})\in U
and
(x,y)\in G^{2}
such that
w_{1}\sim^{0}x
and
w_{2}\sim^{0}y
and
w_{3}\sim^{0}x+y
, and
w_{i}
is generic in
W_{i}
.

Let
V
,
W_{1}
, and
W_{2}
be irreducible complex varieties,
and let
U\subseteq W_{1}\times V\times W_{2}
be an irreducible subvariety.

Definition 8.3
.

Let
D\subseteq V
be a finite subset and
\varepsilon>0
. We call
D

\varepsilon
-abelian
in
U
if there are irreducible complex subvarieties
V^{\prime}\subseteq V
and
U^{\prime}\subseteq U
such that
U^{\prime}\subseteq W_{1}\times V^{\prime}\times W_{2}
is in co-ordinatewise correspondence with the graph of addition of a commutative algebraic group
G
, and
|D\cap V^{\prime}|\geq|D|^{1-\varepsilon}
.
4
4

4

Note that if
v\in V^{\prime}
and the fibre
U_{v}
is a generically finite algebraic correspondence between
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
, we have
U^{\prime}_{v}=U_{v}
. But in general,
U^{\prime}
could be a proper subset of
U\cap W_{1}\times V^{\prime}\times W_{2}
.

In these terms, our main result Theorem
7.20
translates to the
following finitary statement:

Theorem 8.4
.

Let
\tilde{V}
be a constructible subset of
V
.
Suppose for all
v\in\tilde{V}
,

U_{v}:=\{(x,y):(x,v,y)\in U\}\subseteq W_{1}\times W_{2}
is a
generically finite algebraic correspondence,
and
U_{v^{\prime}}=U_{v}
for only finitely many
v^{\prime}\in V
.

Then for all
\varepsilon>0
there exists
\eta>0

such that the following holds.

Let
D\subseteq\tilde{V}
be a finite subset which is not
\varepsilon
-abelian in
U
,
and let
A\subseteq W_{1}
and
B\subseteq W_{2}
be finite subsets such that:

(i)

|A|=|B|\geq\frac{1}{\eta}
.

(ii)

|A|^{\varepsilon}\leq|D|\leq|A|^{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}
.

(iii)

If
W_{1}^{\prime}\subsetneq W_{1}
is a proper irreducible complex subvariety of
complexity
5
5

5

The notion of complexity can be any which takes values in
{\mathbb{N}}
and is such that for any
\tau
, the family of subvarieties of
W_{1}
of complexity
\leq\tau
is a constructible family; see
[
1
, §2.1.10]
for an explicit definition.

\leq\frac{1}{\eta}
then

|A\cap W_{1}^{\prime}|\leq|A|^{\eta}
.
Similarly for
(B,W_{2})
in place of
(A,W_{1})
.

Then
|U\cap(A\times D\times B)|\leq(|A||D|)^{1-\eta}
.

Proof.

Let
\varepsilon>0
, suppose no such
\eta>0
exists.
For
\eta=\frac{1}{n}
, let
D_{n},A_{n},B_{n}
be a counterexample.
Let
D
be the ultraproduct
D=\prod_{n\to{\mathcal{U}}}D_{n}\subseteq V(K)
, where
K={\mathbb{C}}^{\mathcal{U}}
, and define
A
and
B
similarly.

Then
|A|\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{U}}\setminus{\mathbb{N}}
by (i), so let
\boldsymbol{\delta}:=\boldsymbol{\delta}_{|A|}
.

We have
\boldsymbol{\delta}(U\cap(A\times D\times B))\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)
(since
\boldsymbol{\delta}(U\cap(A\times D\times B))\geq(1-\frac{1}{n})(\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(D))
for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{>0}
by assumption), and

\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(B)
. Note that
0<\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)<\infty
by (ii).

Work in an adequate expansion of
(K;+,\cdot)
satisfying Assumption
5.1
in which
A,B,D
are
\emptyset
-definable and
W_{1},V,W_{2},U,\tilde{V}
are defined over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
, which exists by Lemma
4.1
.

Find
(a,d,b)\in U\cap(A\times D\times B)
such that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(adb)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(U\cap(A\times D\times B))
.
If
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/d)>0
then
it follows from (iii) that
{\operatorname{tp}}(a)
is cgp, and
{\operatorname{loc}}^{0}(a/{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(d))=W_{1}
; indeed,
if
W_{1}^{\prime}=\prod_{m\to{\mathcal{U}}}(W_{1}^{\prime})_{m}
is a proper irreducible subvariety of
W_{1}
over
K
, then by (iii),
\{m:|A_{m}\cap(W_{1}^{\prime})_{m}|\leq|A_{m}|^{\frac{1}{m}}\}\in{\mathcal{U}}
, and so
\boldsymbol{\delta}(A\cap W_{1}^{\prime})=0
. This shows that
{\operatorname{tp}}(a)
is cgp, and then from
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/d)>0
we conclude
{\operatorname{loc}}^{0}(a/{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(d))=W_{1}
.
Then
a\sim^{0}_{d}b
since
U_{d}
is a generically finite algebraic correspondence,
so
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/d)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/d)>0
.

By symmetry, we obtain that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/d)>0
if and only if
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/d)>0
.
If
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/d)=0=\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/d)
then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(adb)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)
, contradicting
\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)>0
.
So we conclude that
{\operatorname{tp}}(a)
and
{\operatorname{tp}}(b)
are cgp and broad, and
a\sim^{0}_{d}b
.

Now since

\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
and

\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)
,
we have
\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(U\cap(A\times D\times B))=\boldsymbol{\delta}(abd)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(ad)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)
,
so
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}d
, and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)>0
.
Similarly,
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}d
.

We may assume
{\operatorname{tp}}(d)
is wgp by Lemma
5.8
(i); this may increase
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
, but we still have
{\operatorname{loc}}^{0}(a/{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(d))=W_{1}
by (iii) since
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/d)>0
.
Hence also
{\operatorname{loc}}^{0}(a)=W_{1}
, and similarly
{\operatorname{loc}}^{0}(b)=W_{2}
.

Now
d
is interalgebraic with
cb^{0}(ab/d)
by the assumption that
U_{v^{\prime}}=U_{d}
for only finitely many
v^{\prime}\in V
.

Let
V^{\prime}=loc^{0}(d)
. Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(D\cap V^{\prime})\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)
.

By Theorem
7.20
,
(a,d,b)
is abelian. By the remark in the last paragraph of Definition
8.2
, there is a commutative algebraic group
G
such that
{\operatorname{loc}}^{0}(a,d,b)\subseteq W_{1}\times V^{\prime}\times W_{2}

is in co-ordinatewise correspondence with the graph of addition of
G
.

Now
U^{\prime}:={\operatorname{loc}}^{0}(a,d,b)
is a subset of
U\cap(W_{1}\times V^{\prime}\times W_{2})
. But no
D_{n}
is
\varepsilon
-abelian in
U
, so we must have
\boldsymbol{\delta}(D\cap V^{\prime})\leq(1-\varepsilon)\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)
, contradicting
\boldsymbol{\delta}(D\cap V^{\prime})\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)
.
∎

We deduce a somewhat simpler statement by introducing additional algebraic
assumptions:

Corollary 8.5
.

Suppose now
\dim(W_{1})=\dim(W_{2})=\dim(V)=:k
, and

\dim(U)=2k
and
U
projects dominantly to each pair
W_{1}\times V
,
V\times W_{2}
, and
W_{1}\times W_{2}
, and suppose
U
is not in co-ordinatewise correspondence with the graph of addition of a commutative algebraic group.

Then for all
\varepsilon>0
there exists
\eta>0
such that
if
A\subseteq W_{1}
and
B\subseteq W_{2}
and
D\subseteq V
are finite
subsets such that for each
d\in D
, the fibre
U_{d}
is a generically finite
algebraic correspondence between
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
,
and
U_{d}=U_{v}
holds for only finitely many
v\in V
,
and (i)-(iii) of Theorem
8.4
hold,
then
|U\cap(A\times D\times B)|\leq(|A||D|)^{1-\eta}
.

Proof.

Let
\tilde{V}
be the set of
v\in V
for which
U_{v}
is a generically finite algebraic correspondence between
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
, and
U_{v}=U_{v^{\prime}}
holds for only finitely many
v^{\prime}\in V
.
Then
\tilde{V}
is constructible. Indeed, the finiteness condition is definable by uniform finiteness, and the correspondence condition holds of
U_{v}
if and only if it is irreducible and
\dim(U_{v})=\dim(\pi_{W_{1}}(U_{v}))=\dim(\pi_{W_{2}}(U_{v}))=k
.

The result then follows from Theorem
8.4
once we observe that
D
is not
\varepsilon
-abelian. But indeed, suppose
U^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime}
witness that
D
is
\varepsilon
-abelian. Then
\dim U^{\prime}=2\dim(W_{1})=\dim(U)
, so
U^{\prime}=U
(and
V^{\prime}=V
), contradicting the assumption that
U
is not in co-ordinatewise correspondence with the graph of addition of a commutative algebraic group.
∎

Specialising further, we obtain an unbalanced generalisation of the 1-dimensional
case of the main theorem of
[
9
]
, and in this case we can bound
the exponent using the results of Appendix
A
:

Theorem 8.6
.

Let
U\subseteq W_{1}\times V\times W_{2}
be as in
Corollary
8.5
with
k=1
.

Let
c
be the constant from Fact
A.11
.
Replacing
c
with
\min(1,c)
if necessary, we may assume
c\leq 1
.
Let
\varepsilon_{0}:=\varepsilon 2^{-(\frac{4}{c\varepsilon}+7)}

and let
\eta_{0}:=\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{1+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}
.

Then for all
1>\varepsilon>0
there exists
\eta>0
such that the following holds.
If
A\subseteq W_{1}
,
B\subseteq W_{2}
, and
D\subseteq V
are finite
subsets satisfying conditions
(i)-(ii) of Theorem
8.4
,
then
|U\cap(A\times D\times B)|\leq(|A||D|)^{1-\eta_{0}}
.

Proof.

Otherwise, as in the proof of Theorem
8.4
,
we obtain
(a,d,b)\in A\times D\times B

with
\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)=1=\dim(V)
(by setting
\boldsymbol{\delta}=\boldsymbol{\delta}_{|D|}
)
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(B)\in[\varepsilon,\frac{1}{\varepsilon}]

and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(adb)\geq(1-\eta_{0})(\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)+1)
.

Note
\eta_{0}\leq\varepsilon 2^{-7}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2\varepsilon+2}
,
so

\boldsymbol{\delta}(adb)\geq(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{2(\varepsilon+1)})(\varepsilon+1)=\varepsilon+1-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}>1
.

We may also assume that
D
omits the finitely many elements
v\in V
for
which
U_{v}
is not a correspondence.
Note that for all
v\in V
,
U_{v}=U_{v^{\prime}}
for only finitely many
v^{\prime}
; indeed, otherwise it would hold for all but finitely many
v^{\prime}
, and then the projection to
W_{1}\times W_{2}
of
U
would be a finite union of fibres
U_{v_{i}}
so has dimension
\leq 1
, contrary to assumption.
We do not have
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/d)=0=\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/d)
, since
\boldsymbol{\delta}(adb)>1\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)
.
So (as in Theorem
8.4
)

\dim^{0}(a/d)=1=\dim^{0}(b/d)
and
a\sim^{0}_{d}b
, and
d\sim^{0}{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(ab/d)
.

We proceed to show that
(a,d,b)
is an
(\varepsilon,1)
-correspondence triangle (see Definition
A.5
).

Now
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/d)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(adb)-\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)\geq(1-\eta_{0})(\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)+1)-\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)-\eta_{0}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+1)+1-\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)
,
so

\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)-\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/d)}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)}\leq 1-\frac{1-\eta_{0}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+1)}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)}=1-\frac{1-\varepsilon_{0}}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)}\leq\varepsilon_{0}

(using
\eta_{0}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+1)=\varepsilon_{0}
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)\leq 1
).

Note that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\delta}(adb)-\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/d)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(adb)-\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\eta_{0})(\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)+1)-\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)=1-\eta_{0}(\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)+1)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\eta_{0}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+1)=1-\varepsilon_{0}>\frac{1}{2},

and

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\delta}(ad)-\boldsymbol{\delta}(d/a)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(adb)-\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\eta_{0})(\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)+1)-\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)-\eta_{0}(\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)+1)

\displaystyle\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)-\eta_{0}(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+1)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)-\varepsilon_{0}>\frac{\varepsilon}{2}~(\text{since }\varepsilon_{0}<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}).

Thus,
\frac{1}{2}<\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)=1
and
\frac{\varepsilon}{2}<\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}
and
\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)}\in(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{2}{\varepsilon})
.

Rescaling by working with
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{\xi^{\prime}}
instead of
\boldsymbol{\delta}=\boldsymbol{\delta}_{\xi}=\boldsymbol{\delta}_{|D|}
where
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{\xi^{\prime}}(d)=1
(and hence
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{\xi^{\prime}}(t)=\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)}\boldsymbol{\delta}(t)
for all
t\in K^{n}
), we may assume
\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)=1
. Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)-\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/d)\leq\varepsilon_{0}

and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)\in(\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{2}{\varepsilon})
.

Let
i_{0}:=1+\lfloor\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)\rfloor\leq 1+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}
and note
that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)2^{-(\frac{2\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{c}+7)}>\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\cdot 2^{-(\frac{4}{c\varepsilon}+7)}\geq\varepsilon_{0}
.
We also have

\varepsilon_{0}\leq 2^{-(\frac{4}{c\varepsilon}+8)}\leq 2^{-(2i_{0}+4)}\leq\min(2^{-(i_{0}+1)},\frac{1}{20})
(using
c\leq 1
).

Then Proposition
A.14
case (1), with
\gamma=0
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}=1
,
applies to show that
(a,d,b)
is abelian, leading to a contradiction as in
Corollary
8.5
.
∎

9.
Expansion under polynomial families

In this section, we prove our generalisation of the Elekes-Rónyai theorem.

We write
{\operatorname{AGL}}_{1}
for the 2-dimensional algebraic group of affine linear transformations of
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, which we identify with the semidirect product
{\operatorname{AGL}}_{1}={\mathbb{G}}_{a}\rtimes{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
where
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
acts on
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}
by multiplication.

Fact 9.1
.

The proper non-trivial connected algebraic subgroups
H\leq{\operatorname{AGL}}_{1}
are precisely
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\leq{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\rtimes{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
and the conjugates within
{\operatorname{AGL}}_{1}
of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\leq{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\rtimes{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

Proof.

The image of
H
under the homomorphism
\pi_{2}:{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\rtimes{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

is either trivial,
in which case
H={\mathbb{G}}_{a}
, or the whole of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.
In the latter case, say
(a,b)\in H
where
b\in{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
is not a root of unity.
Setting
\gamma:=(\frac{a}{b-1},1)
, we have
\gamma^{-1}(0,b)\gamma=(a,b)
, so

(a,b)\in({\mathbb{G}}_{m})^{\gamma}
,
so
H
intersects
({\mathbb{G}}_{m})^{\gamma}
on the infinite subgroup generated by
(a,b)
.
But
\dim(H)=1
, so then
H=({\mathbb{G}}_{m})^{\gamma}
.
∎

For a field
k
and a natural number
d
, we write
k[t]_{\leq d}
for the set
of polynomials over
k
of degree at most
d
.

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
f\in K[t]
, and suppose
(a,f,f(a))
is a correspondence triangle for some
a\in K
(where recall we identify
f
with its tuple of coefficients).
Then there are
h_{0},g\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)[t]
and
d\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f)
such that

f(x)=h_{0}(g(x)+_{G}d)
, where
+_{G}
is either addition or multiplication.

Proof.

Let
C_{0}
be the subfield
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)\leq K
.
Let
b:=f(a)
. The correspondence triangle
(a,f,b)
is semi-cgp, since
\dim^{0}(a)=1
and so
a
is cgp by Remark
5.7
.

By Theorem
7.20
,

\dim^{0}(f)=\dim^{0}(a)=1
,
and taking
f^{\prime}b^{\prime}\equiv_{a}fb
with

f^{\prime}b^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{a}fb
(and hence
f^{\prime}b^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{a}fb
via Fact
5.9
(ii)),
and setting
e:={\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(bb^{\prime}/ff^{\prime})
,
we have
\dim^{0}(e)=1
and
f\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}e
.

The remainder of this proof uses ideas and lemmas from
[
8
]
.

Let
s:=\deg(f)=\deg(f^{\prime})
.
Let
g^{\prime}\in K[x]
be of maximal degree such that

\exists l,l^{\prime}\in K[x]_{\leq s}\;(f=l\circ g^{\prime}\wedge f^{\prime}=l^{\prime}\circ g^{\prime})
.

Claim 9.3
.

There exist
g\in C_{0}[x]

and
\hat{f}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f)[x]

and
\hat{f}^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f^{\prime})[x]

such that
f=\hat{f}\circ g
and
f^{\prime}=\hat{f}^{\prime}\circ g

and
\deg(g)=\deg(g^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Consider the definable equivalence relation on
K[x]_{\leq s}

defined by
h\sim h^{\prime}
if there exists a non-constant linear polynomial
