\alpha\in K[x]
such
that
h^{\prime}=\alpha\circ h
.

By
[
8
, Proposition 9]
,
\{h\in K[x]_{\leq s}:\exists l\in K[x]_{\leq s}\;f=l\circ h\}
is a union of
\leq 2^{s}

\sim
-equivalence classes,
so
[g^{\prime}]_{\sim}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f)
.

Similarly
[g^{\prime}]_{\sim}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f^{\prime})
. But
f\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}f^{\prime}
, so
[g^{\prime}]_{\sim}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f)\cap{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f^{\prime})={\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)=C_{0}
; in other words, the constructible set
[g^{\prime}]_{\sim}
is defined over
C_{0}
.
Since
C_{0}\leq K
is an algebraically closed subfield, there exists
g\in[g^{\prime}]_{\sim}\cap C_{0}[x]_{\leq s}
.

Finally, we can take
\hat{f}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f)[x]
and
\hat{f}^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f^{\prime})[x]

such that
f=\hat{f}\circ g
and
f^{\prime}=\hat{f}^{\prime}\circ g
,
by elementarity of
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f)
resp.
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f^{\prime})
in
K
.
∎
9.3

Now let
\tilde{f}\tilde{f}^{\prime}\tilde{\hat{f}}\tilde{\hat{f}}^{\prime}\tilde{b}\tilde{b}^{\prime}\equiv^{0}_{e}ff^{\prime}\hat{f}\hat{f}^{\prime}bb^{\prime}
with
\tilde{f}\tilde{f}^{\prime}\tilde{\hat{f}}\tilde{\hat{f}}^{\prime}\tilde{b}\tilde{b}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{e}ff^{\prime}\hat{f}\hat{f}^{\prime}bb^{\prime}

Let
\gamma
be the Zariski closure in
{\mathbb{A}}^{2}
of

\{(\hat{f}(x),\hat{f}^{\prime}(x)):x\in{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\}=(\hat{f}\times\hat{f}^{\prime})({\mathbb{A}}^{1})
.
Then
\gamma={\operatorname{loc}}^{0}(bb^{\prime}/{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(ff^{\prime}))
, since
\gamma
is irreducible,
and
\dim^{0}(bb^{\prime}/ff^{\prime})=1=\dim\gamma
,
and
(b,b^{\prime})=(\hat{f}(g(a)),\hat{f}^{\prime}(g(a)))\in\gamma
.
So by choice of
e
, also
\gamma={\operatorname{loc}}^{0}(bb^{\prime}/e)
, and
\gamma
is defined
over
{\operatorname{dcl}}^{0}(e)
.
Hence
\gamma
is also the Zariski closure of
(\tilde{\hat{f}}\times\tilde{\hat{f}}^{\prime})({\mathbb{A}}^{1})
.

So by
[
8
, Lemma 12]
,
there are polynomials
h,h^{\prime},\phi_{f},\phi_{\tilde{f}}\in K[x]

such that

\displaystyle\hat{f}

\displaystyle=h\circ\phi_{f}

\displaystyle\hat{f}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=h^{\prime}\circ\phi_{f}

\displaystyle\tilde{\hat{f}}

\displaystyle=h\circ\phi_{\tilde{f}}.

Then
\phi_{f}
is linear by maximality of
\deg(g)
,
since
f=\hat{f}\circ g=h\circ\phi_{f}\circ g

and
f^{\prime}=\hat{f}^{\prime}\circ g=h^{\prime}\circ\phi_{f}\circ g
.

We now proceed as in the proof of the claim.
Note that
f\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}\tilde{f}
(since
f\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}e
), and hence
\hat{f}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}\tilde{\hat{f}}
.
Consider the definable equivalence relation on
K[x]_{\leq s}

defined by
h\sim_{\operatorname{out}}h^{\prime}
if there exists a non-constant linear polynomial

\alpha\in K[x]
such that
h^{\prime}=h\circ\alpha
.
By
[
8
, Proposition 9]
(see the remark at the end of
[
8
, Definition 8]
),

\{\theta\in K[x]_{\leq s}:\exists l\in K[x]_{\leq s}\;\hat{f}=\theta\circ l\}
is a union of
\leq 2^{s}

\sim_{\operatorname{out}}
-equivalence classes,
so
[h]_{\sim_{\operatorname{out}}}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\hat{f})
, and similarly
[h]_{\sim_{\operatorname{out}}}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\tilde{\hat{f}})
,
so
[h]_{\sim_{\operatorname{out}}}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\hat{f})\cap{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\tilde{\hat{f}})=C_{0}
,
so there is a non-constant linear polynomial
\alpha\in K[x]

such that
h_{0}:=h\circ\alpha\in C_{0}[x]
.

So we have decomposed
f
as
f=h_{0}\circ\alpha^{-1}\circ\phi_{f}\circ g
with
h_{0},g\in C_{0}[x]
and

\alpha^{-1}\circ\phi_{f}
linear, and so by elementarity of
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f)
in

K
, we may find a linear polynomial
\phi_{f}^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f)[x]

such that
f=h_{0}\circ\phi_{f}^{\prime}\circ g
.

Now
a^{\prime}:=g(a)\sim^{0}a
and
\phi_{f}^{\prime}\sim^{0}f
and
b^{\prime}:=\phi_{f}^{\prime}(g(a))\sim^{0}b
. So
(a^{\prime},\phi_{f}^{\prime},b^{\prime})
is a cgp correspondence triangle,
and satisfies the conditions of
Corollary
5.22

for the action of
{\operatorname{AGL}}_{1}={\mathbb{G}}_{a}\rtimes{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
on
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
.
Hence
\phi_{f}^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{AGL}}_{1}
is generic in a left coset
X
defined over
C_{0}
of a connected abelian subgroup
G^{\prime}\leq{\operatorname{AGL}}_{1}
.
Since
X
is over
C_{0}
, also
G^{\prime}
is over
C_{0}
.
So by Fact
9.1
,
G^{\prime}
is either
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\leq{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\rtimes{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

or a conjugate by some
\alpha\in{\operatorname{AGL}}_{1}(C_{0})
of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\leq{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\rtimes{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

Therefore, after composing
h_{0}
and
g
with linear polynomials over

C_{0}
, we obtain
f=h_{0}\circ\phi^{\prime}_{f}\circ g
where
\phi^{\prime}_{f}
is either
addition or multiplication by a constant.
∎

For a finite set
F\subseteq{\mathbb{C}}[t]_{\leq d}
and
\varepsilon>0
we call
F

\varepsilon
-additive
, if there are
g,h\in{\mathbb{C}}[t]
such that

|F\cap\{h(g(t)+a):a\in{\mathbb{C}}\}|\geq|F|^{1-\varepsilon}.

Similarly, we call
F

\varepsilon
-multiplicative
, if there are
g,h\in{\mathbb{C}}[t]
such that

|F\cap\{h(g(t)\cdot a):a\in{\mathbb{C}}\}|\geq|F|^{1-\varepsilon}.

Theorem 9.4
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\eta>0

such that
for any finite set
F\subseteq{\mathbb{C}}[t]
of non-constant polynomials of degree

\leq 1/\varepsilon
which is neither
\varepsilon
-additive nor
\varepsilon
-multiplicative,
for any finite set
A\subseteq{\mathbb{C}}

with
|F|\geq|A|^{\varepsilon}\geq\frac{1}{\eta}
,
we have

|F*A|=|\{f(a):f\in F,\;a\in A\}|\geq|A|^{1+\eta}.

Proof.

Let
\varepsilon>0
, and suppose there is no such
\eta
.
Let
d:=\lceil\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\rceil
.
Taking an ultraproduct of counterexamples
(A_{n},F_{n})
with
\eta=\frac{1}{n}
, we obtain infinite pseudofinite sets
A\subseteq K={\mathbb{C}}^{\mathcal{U}}
and

F\subseteq K[t]_{\leq d}
such that,
with
\boldsymbol{\delta}=\boldsymbol{\delta}_{|A|}
,

•

\boldsymbol{\delta}(F)\geq\varepsilon\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)

•

\boldsymbol{\delta}(F*A)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)

(where
\boldsymbol{\delta}(F*A)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
holds since if
f\in F
, we have
|A|\leq d|f*A|
, since
f(x)=b
has
\leq d
solutions in
x
for any
b
),
and for any
g,h\in K[t]
with
\deg g+\deg h\leq d
,
setting
V_{g,h}^{+}:=\{h(g(t)+a):a\in K\}\subseteq K[t]_{\leq d}

and
V_{g,h}^{\cdot}:=\{h(g(t)\cdot a):a\in K\}\subseteq K[t]_{\leq d}
,
we have
\boldsymbol{\delta}(F\cap V_{g,h}^{+})\leq(1-\varepsilon)\boldsymbol{\delta}(F)

and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(F\cap V_{g,h}^{\cdot})\leq(1-\varepsilon)\boldsymbol{\delta}(F)
.
Note that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(F)<\infty
. Indeed, take
Y\subseteq A
of size
d+1
. If
f(y)=g(y)
for all
y\in Y
, then
f=g
. Therefore,
|(F*Y)^{d+1}|\geq|F|
. Hence,
\boldsymbol{\delta}(F)\leq(d+1)\boldsymbol{\delta}(F*A)=(d+1)\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
.

Work in an adequate expansion of
(K;+,\cdot)
satisfying Assumption
5.1
in which
A
and
F
are
\emptyset
-definable, which exists by Lemma
4.1
.
Take
(a,f)\in A\times F

with
\boldsymbol{\delta}(af)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(A\times F)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(F)
,
where we identify a polynomial in
K[t]_{\leq d}
with the element of

K^{d+1}
consisting of its coefficients.
Then
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}f
.
Set
b:=f(a)\in F*A
,
so (since
f
is a non-constant polynomial)
a\sim^{0}_{f}b
,
and
f\sim^{0}{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(ab/f)
by the identification of
f
with its
coefficients.
Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(F*A)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/f)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/f)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(b)
,
so
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}f
.

Now by Lemma
5.8
(i), let
c
be such that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(c)=0
and

{\operatorname{tp}}(f/c)
is wgp. Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab/fc)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab/f)>0
so
\dim^{0}(ab/fc)>0
, so
\dim^{0}(ab/fc)=1=\dim^{0}(ab/f)
,
so
ab\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{f}c
.
Also
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}f\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}b
since
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/f),\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/f)>0
.
By Lemma
7.18
,
{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}_{c}(ab/f)\sim^{0}_{c}f
.

Add
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(c)
to the language. Then
{\operatorname{tp}}(f)
is wgp and broad,
and
{\operatorname{tp}}(a)
is broad and cgp since
\dim^{0}(a)=1
(see Remark
5.7
),
and by the above we still have
f\sim^{0}{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(ab/f)
.

Therefore,
(a,f,b)
is a semi-cgp correspondence triangle.

By Lemma
9.2
,

f\in V_{g,h_{0}}^{+}
or
f\in V_{g,h_{0}}^{\cdot}
.
But then since
V_{g,h_{0}}^{+}
and
V_{g,h_{0}}^{\cdot}
are over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
,
we have

(1-\varepsilon)\boldsymbol{\delta}(F)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(F\cap V_{g,h_{0}}^{+})\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(f)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(F)
,
which is a contradiction.
∎

Remark 9.5
.

The bound on degree in Theorem
9.4
is necessary:
for example,
taking
F:=\{x^{2^{i}}:0<i\leq n\}
and
A:=\{2^{2^{i}}:i\leq n\}
, we have

F*A=\{2^{2^{j}}:0<j\leq 2n\}
.

Remark 9.6
.

We leave it to the interested reader to verify a version of
Theorem
9.4
in the case of rational functions
F\subseteq{\mathbb{C}}(x)
, where
{\operatorname{PSL}}_{2}
will appear in place of
{\operatorname{AGL}}_{1}
in the proof.

We now proceed to deduce our arbitrarily unbalanced version of Elekes-Rónyai.

Definition 9.7
.

A polynomial
f\in{\mathbb{C}}[x,\bar{y}]

is
additive
if there
exist
g,h\in{\mathbb{C}}[x]
and
s\in{\mathbb{C}}[\bar{y}]
such that
f(x,\bar{y})=g(h(x)+s(\bar{y}))
, and

multiplicative
if there
exist
g,h\in{\mathbb{C}}[x]
and
s\in{\mathbb{C}}[\bar{y}]
such that
f(x,\bar{y})=g(h(x)\cdot s(\bar{y}))
.

Theorem 9.8
.

Suppose
f\in{\mathbb{C}}[x,{\bar{y}}]
is neither additive nor multiplicative. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, there are
C,\eta>0
such that for any finite
A,B_{0},\ldots,B_{|{\bar{y}}|-1}\subseteq{\mathbb{C}}
with
|A|\geq|B_{i}|\geq|A|^{\varepsilon}\geq C
for each
i<|{\bar{y}}|
, we have
|f(A,B_{0},\ldots,B_{|{\bar{y}}|-1})|\geq|A|^{1+\eta}
.

Moreover, one can take
\eta:=|{\bar{y}}|^{-2}\cdot 2^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{\varepsilon}}
for some absolute constant
c^{\prime}
; in particular,
\eta
depends only on
|{\bar{y}}|
and
\varepsilon
.

Proof.

Let
f\in{\mathbb{C}}[x,{\bar{y}}]
and
\varepsilon>0
be given.
We first prove the qualitative statement that
C,\eta>0
exist, then consider how to adapt the argument to obtain
\eta
.

Let
m:=|{\bar{y}}|
.
Suppose no such
C,\eta>0
exist.
By taking an ultraproduct of counter-examples of growing sizes for decreasing
\eta
, we find internal subsets
A,B_{0},\ldots,B_{m-1}\subseteq K
such that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
|f(A,B_{0},\ldots,B_{m-1})|<|A|^{1+\frac{1}{n}}
and
|A|\geq|B_{i}|\geq|A|^{\varepsilon}\geq n
for all
i<m
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\delta}:=\boldsymbol{\delta}_{\max\{|B_{i}|:i<m\}}
.
Take
(a,{\bar{b}})\in A\times B_{0}\times\ldots\times B_{m-1}
such that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a{\bar{b}})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(A\times B_{0}\times\ldots\times B_{m-1})
.
Then
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}{\bar{b}}
, and
{\bar{b}}=(b_{i})_{i<m}
is a
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}
-independent sequence.
Note that
\max\{\boldsymbol{\delta}(b_{i}):i<m\}=1
and
1\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b_{i})\geq\varepsilon\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)
.
In particular,
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a),\boldsymbol{\delta}(b_{i})>0
. Hence
(a,{\bar{b}})
is a generic tuple in
K^{m+1}
over
C_{0}:={\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
.

Let
f_{\bar{b}}:=f(x,{\bar{b}})\in K[x]
and
b:=f_{\bar{b}}(a)
.
Then
f_{\bar{b}}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}({\bar{b}})
(identifying
f_{\bar{b}}
with the sequence of its coefficients), and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}f_{\bar{b}}
.
Note that
f_{\bar{b}}
is non-constant, since
f(x,\bar{y})
is not additive and so depends on
x
, and
{\bar{b}}
is generic in
K^{m}
.
So
b\sim^{0}_{f_{\bar{b}}}a
.

Now
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)
, so
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/f_{\bar{b}})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/f_{\bar{b}})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(b)
and so
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}f_{\bar{b}}
.
Since
{\bar{b}}
is
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}
-independent and each
b_{i}
is cgp (by
\dim^{0}(b_{i})=1
and Remark
5.7
),
{\bar{b}}
and hence
f_{\bar{b}}
are wgp by Fact
5.9
and Remark
5.10
.
So
(a,f_{\bar{b}},b)
is a semi-cgp correspondence triangle,
hence by Lemma
9.2
, there are
h_{0},g\in C_{0}[t]
and
d\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}({\bar{b}})

such that
f_{\bar{b}}(x)=h_{0}(g(x)+_{G}d)
, where
+_{G}
is either addition or multiplication.

Now we have
a\sim^{0}g(a)
,
b\sim^{0}g(a)+_{G}d
and
b\in{\operatorname{dcl}}^{0}(a,{\bar{b}})
.
We follow the argument of Remark
7.6
and claim that the minimal polynomial
m(t)
of
d
over
C_{0}(a,{\bar{b}})
is
t^{k}-d^{k}
for some
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Indeed, let
\sigma\in{\operatorname{Gal}}(C_{0}(a,{\bar{b}}))
, and let
\xi:=\sigma(d)-_{G}d\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}({\bar{b}})
.
Let
g_{b}:=g(a)+_{G}d\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(b)
.
Then
\sigma(g_{b})=\sigma(g(a)+_{G}d)=g(a)+_{G}\sigma(d)=g_{b}+_{G}\xi
, and since
b\in C_{0}(a,{\bar{b}})
and
g_{b}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(b)
, we have
\sigma(g_{b})\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(b)
and so
\xi=\sigma(g_{b})-_{G}g_{b}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(b)
.
But
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}{\bar{b}}
; indeed, since
f_{\bar{b}}
is non-constant we have
a\sim^{0}_{\bar{b}}b
, and so
\dim^{0}(b/{\bar{b}})=\dim^{0}(a/{\bar{b}})=1=\dim^{0}(b)
.
So
\xi\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(b)\cap{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}({\bar{b}})={\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)=C_{0}
.
Hence the roots of
m(t)
are invariant under
(x\mapsto x+_{G}\xi)
.
Considering arbitrary
\sigma
, we conclude that the roots of
m(t)
are of the form
d+_{G}\Xi
for the finite subgroup
\Xi=\{\sigma(d)-_{G}d:\sigma\in{\operatorname{Gal}}(C_{0}(a,{\bar{b}}))\}
.
If
+_{G}
is addition, then
\Xi
is trivial as
\operatorname{char}(K)=0
, so
m(t)=t-d
as required. If
+_{G}
is multiplication, then the roots are of the form
\{d\cdot\zeta^{i}:i<k\}
where
\zeta
is a primitive
k
th root of unity, and then
m(t)=t^{k}-d^{k}
as required.

Continue to assume
+_{G}
is multiplication.
Say
h_{0}(t)=\sum_{i\leq n}a_{i}t^{i}
.
Then we can write
b=h_{0}(g(a)\cdot d)
as

b=\sum_{i\leq n}a_{i}g(a)^{i}d^{i-\lfloor\frac{i}{k}\rfloor k}\cdot d^{\lfloor\frac{i}{k}\rfloor k}.

Let
d_{i}:=d^{\lfloor\frac{i}{k}\rfloor k}\in C_{0}(\bar{b})
.
Then

\sum_{i\leq n}a_{i}g(a)^{i}d_{i}t^{i-\lfloor\frac{i}{k}\rfloor k}-b=0

is a polynomial in
C_{0}(a,{\bar{b}})
of degree
<k
satisfied by
d
.
Thus, it is the constant zero polynomial, and
a_{i}=0
for all
i
not divisible by
k
(as
g(a)\neq 0)
.
Therefore,
h_{0}(t)=\hat{h}_{0}(t^{k})
for some
\hat{h}_{0}\in C_{0}[t]
and
f(a,{\bar{b}})=\hat{h}_{0}(g(a)^{k}\cdot d^{k})
.

Meanwhile in the additive case, we have
k=1
and
f(a,{\bar{b}})=h_{0}(g(a)+d)
.
But
d^{k}\in C_{0}(a,{\bar{b}})\cap{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}({\bar{b}})=C_{0}({\bar{b}})
and
(a,{\bar{b}})
is generic,
so in both cases we have an equality of rational functions
f(x,{\bar{y}})=h^{\prime}(g^{\prime}(x)+_{G}s({\bar{y}}))
where
h^{\prime}
and
g^{\prime}
are non-constant polynomials over
C_{0}
and
s({\bar{y}})\in C_{0}({\bar{y}})
.
But then since
f
and
h^{\prime}
are polynomials, so must be
g^{\prime}(x)+_{G}s({\bar{y}})
(as can be seen by considering
C_{0}(x,{\bar{y}})
as the fraction field of the unique factorisation domain
C_{0}[x,{\bar{y}}]
), so also
s\in C_{0}[{\bar{y}}]
.
But this contradicts the assumption that
f
is neither additive nor multiplicative.

This concludes the qualitative argument. We now consider the “moreover” clause in the statement. So let
\eta:=m^{-2}\cdot 2^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{\varepsilon}}
, and suppose for a contradiction that we have
a,{\bar{b}},b
as above, but now with
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b)\leq(1+\eta)\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)
rather than
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)
.

We apply Lemma
A.17
from Appendix
A
, using some notation defined in that appendix.

First, since
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+\eta\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)
,
we have
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/f_{\bar{b}})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/f_{\bar{b}})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(b)-\eta\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)
.
Also, since
{\bar{b}}
is a
\boldsymbol{\delta}
-independent sequence with
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b_{i})\geq\varepsilon\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)
, it is easy to see that
{\operatorname{tp}}({\bar{b}})
is
\frac{\varepsilon\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{2}
-wgp.
By Lemma
A.2
(i),
f_{\bar{b}}
is also
\frac{\varepsilon\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{2}
-wgp.

Thus,
(a,f_{\bar{b}},b)
is a semi-cgp
(\eta\boldsymbol{\delta}(a),\frac{\varepsilon\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{2})
-correspondence triangle over
C_{0}:={\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
.
By Lemma
A.17
, there are
h_{0},g\in C_{0}[t]
and an absolute constant
c
such that
f_{\bar{b}}=h_{0}\circ\phi_{f}^{\prime}\circ g
where
\phi_{f}^{\prime}
is either addition or multiplication, provided
\eta\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)<\{\frac{\varepsilon\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{2^{i_{0}+5}m},\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{2^{\frac{2}{c\varepsilon}+7}}\}
where
i_{0}:=1+\lfloor\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\rfloor
.
Choosing an appropriate absolute constant
c^{\prime}
calculated in terms of the absolute constant
c
, we get that
\eta\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)
does satisfy this bound.
∎

Appendix A
Explicit powersaving

In this appendix, we adapt some of our qualitative arguments to obtain bounds on the exponents. The goal of this section is to establish our quantitative asymmetric Elekes–Rónyai Theorem (Theorem
9.8
).
This involves quantitatively weakening our independence and general position assumptions, and tracing how these errors compound through the proofs.
This method works in general. However, some of these results have more restrictive assumptions than the qualitative counterparts, primarily because we do not have a quantitative version of Theorem
5.18
for actions on varieties of dimension
>1
.

A.1.
\varepsilon
-Correspondence triangles

We first give a quantitative analogue of the notion of correspondence triangles. To do so, we weaken the assumption of
\boldsymbol{\delta}
-independence and strengthen the assumption on wgp; these correspond respectively to the expansion constant
\eta
and the lower bound
\epsilon
in the statement of Theorem
9.8
.

Definition A.1
.

Suppose
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/C),\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/C)<\infty
.
Let
\varepsilon\geq 0
.

•

Define
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{C}b
to mean:
\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab/C)+\varepsilon\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/C)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/C)
.
Equivalently,
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/C)-\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/bC)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/C)-\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/aC)\leq\varepsilon
.

•

Say
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/C)
is
\varepsilon
-wgp
if for any tuple
b
such that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{C}b
,
we have
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{C}b
.

•

Say
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/C)
is
\varepsilon
-cgp
if for any tuple
b
such that

a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{C}b
, we have
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/bC)\leq\varepsilon
.

•

Say
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/C)
is
\varepsilon
-broad
if
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/C)>\varepsilon
.

Note that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}
is symmetric, and we have the following versions of transitivity and monotonicity:

a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}bc
implies
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}b
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{b}c
,
and conversely
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}b
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon^{\prime}}_{b}c
imply
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime}}bc
.

Note also that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}Cb
implies
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon’}Cb
whenever
0\leq\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon’
. Moreover, if
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/C)
is
\varepsilon’
-wgp (respectively,
\varepsilon’
-broad), then it is also
\varepsilon
-wgp (respectively,
\varepsilon
-broad), while if
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/C)
is
\varepsilon
-cgp, then it is also
\varepsilon’
-cgp. In particular, if
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/C)
is
\varepsilon
-wgp for some
\varepsilon\geq 0
, then it is wgp.

The following Lemma generalises Fact
5.9
. Here,
\varepsilon
-wgp can be preserved, but sometimes at the cost of replacing
\varepsilon
by a smaller value.

Lemma A.2
.

Suppose
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/C),\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/C)<\infty
.

(i)

If
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/C)
is
\varepsilon
-wgp and
d\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(aC)
, then
{\operatorname{tp}}(d/C)
is
\varepsilon
-wgp.

(ii)

If
{\operatorname{tp}}(b/C)
and
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/Cb)
are
\varepsilon
-wgp, then so is
{\operatorname{tp}}(ab/C)
.

(iii)

If
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{C}b
and
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/C)
is
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-wgp with
\varepsilon^{\prime}\geq\varepsilon
, then
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/Cb)
is
(\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon)
-wgp.

Proof.

(i)

Suppose
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{C}e
.
Let
a^{\prime}\equiv_{Cd}a
with
a^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{Cd}e
.
Then
a^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{C}e
since
d\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(a^{\prime}C)
,
and
{\operatorname{tp}}(a^{\prime}/C)={\operatorname{tp}}(a/C)
is
\varepsilon
-wgp,
so
a^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{C}e
,
and so
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{C}e
;
indeed, if
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{C}e
, then since also
a^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{Cd}e
,
by transitivity with
\varepsilon+0=\varepsilon
we have
a^{\prime}d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{C}e
,
and so in particular
a^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{C}e
.

(ii)

If
ab\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{C}d
then

a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{Cb}d
or
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{C}d
, so

a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{Cb}d
or
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{C}d
by the wgp assumptions,
so
ab\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{C}d
as required.

(iii)

If
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{Cb}d
,
then
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{C}bd
,
so
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon^{\prime}}_{C}bd
by
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-wgp,
so
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon}_{Cb}d
as required.

∎

Definition A.3
.

A sequence
\bar{a}=(a_{i})_{i\in\omega}
is called a
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}
-independent
sequence, if
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a_{i})<\infty
and
a_{i+1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}a_{0}\ldots a_{i}
for
all
i
.

Lemma A.4
.

Suppose
{\bar{a}}=(a_{0},\ldots,a_{k})
is a finite
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}
-independent sequence.
Let
b\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}({\bar{a}})
.

(1)

Assume
{\operatorname{tp}}(a_{i})
is
\gamma
-cgp for all
i
, and

\boldsymbol{\delta}(a_{i})=\dim^{0}(a_{i})
.
Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b)\geq\dim^{0}(b)-k\varepsilon-(k+1)\gamma
.

(2)

Assume
\max\{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a_{i}):i\leq k\}=1=\dim^{0}(a_{i})
for all
i
and
\min\{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a_{i}):i\leq k\}\geq r
for some
r>\varepsilon
. Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b)\geq r\dim^{0}(b)-k\varepsilon
.

Proof.

We first prove item (1).

We have
\boldsymbol{\delta}({\bar{a}})\geq\dim^{0}({\bar{a}})-k\varepsilon
.

We now show that
\boldsymbol{\delta}({\bar{a}}/b)\leq\dim^{0}({\bar{a}}/b)+(k+1)\gamma
(for this we do
not use
b\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}({\bar{a}})
).
For
k=0
: if
{\bar{a}}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}b
then
\boldsymbol{\delta}({\bar{a}}/b)\leq\gamma
by
\gamma
-cgp,
and if
{\bar{a}}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}b
then
\boldsymbol{\delta}({\bar{a}}/b)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}({\bar{a}})=\dim^{0}({\bar{a}})=\dim^{0}({\bar{a}}/b)
.
For
k\geq 1
, we argue by induction. Say
{\bar{a}}={\bar{a}}^{\prime}a
. Then

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\delta}({\bar{a}}/b)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\delta}({\bar{a}}^{\prime}/ba)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/b)

\displaystyle\leq\dim^{0}({\bar{a}}^{\prime}/ba)+k\gamma+\dim^{0}(a/b)+\gamma=\dim^{0}({\bar{a}}/b)+(k+1)\gamma.

So

\boldsymbol{\delta}(b)=\boldsymbol{\delta}({\bar{a}})-\boldsymbol{\delta}({\bar{a}}/b)\geq\dim^{0}({\bar{a}})-\dim^{0}({\bar{a}}/b)-k\varepsilon-(k+1)\gamma=\dim^{0}(b)-k\varepsilon-(k+1)\gamma.

Now we prove item (2).

Note that
\bar{a}
is an
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}
-independent sequence, since if
a_{i}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(a_{j}:j<i)
, then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a_{i}/(a_{j})_{j<i})=0<r-\varepsilon
contradicts
a_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}(a_{j})_{j<i}
.

Let
\bar{a}^{\prime}=(a_{i})_{i\in I^{\prime}}
be a minimal subtuple of
\bar{a}
such that
\bar{a}^{\prime}
is a transcendence base of
\bar{a}
over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(b)
. Then
\dim^{0}(\bar{a}^{\prime})+\dim^{0}(b)=\dim^{0}(\bar{a}^{\prime}/b)+\dim^{0}(b)=\dim^{0}(\bar{a})
, and
\dim^{0}(b)=\dim^{0}(\bar{a})-\dim^{0}(\bar{a}^{\prime})
. As
\bar{a}
is
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}
-independent, we have
|\{0,\ldots,k\}\setminus I^{\prime}|=\dim^{0}(b)
. Hence,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\delta}(b)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\delta}(\bar{a})-\boldsymbol{\delta}(\bar{a}^{\prime}/b)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(\bar{a})-\boldsymbol{\delta}(\bar{a}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i\leq k}\boldsymbol{\delta}(a_{i})-k\varepsilon-\sum_{i\in I^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{\delta}(a_{i})\geq r\dim^{0}(b)-k\varepsilon.\qed

Definition A.5
.

Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}\geq\varepsilon\geq 0
.
Let
C\subseteq K
.
A
coarse
(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence triangle
(or just

(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence triangle
) over
C
is a triple
(a,d,b)
such
that

•

{\operatorname{tp}}(a/C)
is wgp;

•

{\operatorname{tp}}(d/C)
is
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-wgp;

•

a,d\notin{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(C)
;

•

a\sim^{0}_{Cd}b
;

•

d\sim^{0}_{C}{\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(ab/Cd)
;

•

a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{C}d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{C}b
.

If
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/C)
is cgp, we call
(a,d,b)
a
semi-cgp

(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence triangle
over
C
.

In the case
C=\emptyset
, we omit mention of it.

We define abelianity of
(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence triangles exactly as
for correspondence triangles (which are precisely
(0,0)
-correspondence
triangles).

Remark A.6
.

If
(a,d,b)
is an
(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence triangle over
C
then

a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{C}d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{C}b
, since
\varepsilon^{\prime}\geq\varepsilon
.

Remark A.7
.

If
(a,d,b)
is an
(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence triangle over
C
, then

{\operatorname{tp}}(b/C)
is also wgp.

Indeed, since
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{C}a
and
{\operatorname{tp}}(d/C)
is
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-wgp, by
Lemma
A.2
(iii),
{\operatorname{tp}}(d/aC)
is
(\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon)
-wgp, and in
particular is wgp. Since
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/C)
is wgp, also
{\operatorname{tp}}(da/C)
is wgp, hence

{\operatorname{tp}}(b/C)
is also wgp by Lemma
A.2
(i)(ii). Note also that

{\operatorname{tp}}(b/C)
is broad. Recall that for a wgp tuple, broad is equivalent to
non-algebraic. Hence, if
b\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(C)
then
a\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(Cd)
contradicting

a\notin{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(C)
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{C}d
.

Using an explicit Szemerédi-Trotter bound, we get an explicit bound nf

\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)
in terms of
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)
and the algebraic dimension of
d
when

(a,d,b)
is a semi-cgp
(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence triangle.

Lemma A.8
.

Let
(a,d,b)
be a semi-cgp
(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence triangle.
Suppose also that
{\operatorname{tp}}(a/C)
is
3\varepsilon
-broad.
Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+(6\dim^{0}(d)+5)\varepsilon
.

Proof.

We may assume
d={\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(ab/d)
.
Exactly as in the proof of
[
1
, Proposition 5.14]
, applying
Szemerédi-Trotter bounds to the
\bigwedge
-definable binary relation

({\operatorname{tp}}(ab)\times{\operatorname{tp}}(d))\cap{\operatorname{loc}}^{0}(ab,d)
and using that
{\operatorname{tp}}(a)
is cgp,
we obtain

(4)

\boldsymbol{\delta}(abd)\leq\max(\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)-\max(0,\varepsilon_{0}(\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab))),\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab),\boldsymbol{\delta}(d))

where
\varepsilon_{0}>0
.
As in the proof of
[
1
, Lemma 3.11]
, by the primitive element
theorem we may assume that
d
is a
(\dim^{0}(d)+1)
-tuple, and then the
semialgebraic incidence bounds proven in
[
10
]
[Theorem 1.2]
yield that (
4
) holds for any
\varepsilon_{0}<\frac{1}{4(\dim^{0}(d)+1)-1}
,
and hence also for
\varepsilon_{0}=\frac{1}{4(\dim^{0}(d)+1)-1}
.

Suppose for a contradiction that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)>\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+(6\dim^{0}(d)+5)\varepsilon
.
Rearranging, we obtain
\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)>\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}(\frac{3}{\varepsilon_{0}}+1)

where

\varepsilon_{0}=\frac{3}{2(6\dim^{0}(d)+5)-1}=\frac{1}{4(\dim^{0}(d)+1)-1}
, so
(
4
) holds for
\varepsilon_{0}
.

We have
\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/a)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(b)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/d)+\varepsilon=\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/d)+\varepsilon\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+\varepsilon
,
so

\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab)=\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\delta}(b/a)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(a))\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}<\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\frac{3}{\varepsilon_{0}}

and so
\varepsilon_{0}(\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab))>\frac{3\varepsilon}{2}
.
But

\boldsymbol{\delta}(abd)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/d)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)-\varepsilon+\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)\geq\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)-\frac{3\varepsilon}{2},

and

\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)-\frac{3\varepsilon}{2}>\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab)+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab)

(using
\varepsilon_{0}\leq 1
),
and
\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)-\frac{3\varepsilon}{2}>\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)
since

\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\delta}(ab)\geq\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)>\frac{3\varepsilon}{2}
since
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)>3\varepsilon
,
so this contradicts (
4
).
∎

The next two lemmas are quantitative versions of Lemma
7.9
and
Lemma
7.10
respectively.

Lemma A.9
.

Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}\geq 3\varepsilon\geq 0
.
Suppose
(a,d,b)
and
(a^{\prime},d^{\prime},b)
are
(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence
triangles such that

a^{\prime}d^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{b}ad
.
Let
e:={\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(aa^{\prime}/dd^{\prime})
.
Then
(a,e,a^{\prime})
is a
(2\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon)
-correspondence triangle,
and
d\sim^{0}_{e}d^{\prime}
and
\dim^{0}(e)\geq\dim^{0}(d)
.

Proof.

We first verify the conditions for
(a,e,a^{\prime})
to be a
(2\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon)
-correspondence triangle.

•

{\operatorname{tp}}(a)
is wgp and broad by assumption.
We have
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}d^{\prime}
,
and
{\operatorname{tp}}(d)
is
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-wgp,
so
{\operatorname{tp}}(d/d^{\prime})
is
(\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon)
-wgp,
hence so is
{\operatorname{tp}}(dd^{\prime})
and hence
{\operatorname{tp}}(e)
.
We verify below that
{\operatorname{tp}}(e)
is
(\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon)
-broad, and hence
e\notin{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
.

•

a\sim^{0}_{d}b\sim^{0}_{d^{\prime}}a^{\prime}
,
hence
a\sim^{0}_{dd^{\prime}}a^{\prime}
, and so also
a\sim^{0}_{e}a^{\prime}
by choice of

e
.

•

e={\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(aa^{\prime}/e)
by definition.

•

We have

ad\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}d^{\prime}

so
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}_{d}d^{\prime}

and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}d
,
so
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2\varepsilon}dd^{\prime}
,
so
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2\varepsilon}e

since
e\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(dd^{\prime})
,
and similarly
a^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2\varepsilon}e
.

Now we claim that
(d,a,b,d^{\prime},a^{\prime})
satisfies (a) and (b) in
Proposition
6.4
. Condition (a) follows directly
from the assumptions. For (b), we only need to prove

a^{\prime}d^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{b}ad
. By assumption,
