a^{\prime}d^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{b}ad
, hence
d^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{b}d
and

d^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}bd
(as
d^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}b
). Since
{\operatorname{tp}}(d^{\prime})
is
\varepsilon
-wgp, we
have
d^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}bd
and
d^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{b}d
. Since
a\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(bd)
and

a^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(bd^{\prime})
, we conclude
a^{\prime}d^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{b}ad
as desired.

So by Proposition
6.4
,
d^{\prime}\sim^{0}_{e}d
.
Using
e\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(dd^{\prime})
, we deduce
e\sim^{0}_{d^{\prime}}d
.
Since
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}d^{\prime}
, we obtain

\boldsymbol{\delta}(e)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(e/d^{\prime})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(d/d^{\prime})\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)-\varepsilon>\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon
,
and hence
{\operatorname{tp}}(e)
is
(\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon)
-broad.
Finally, the analogous calculation using
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}d^{\prime}
yields

\dim^{0}(e)\geq\dim^{0}(d)
.
∎

Lemma A.10
.

If
(a,d,b)
is a semi-cgp
(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence triangle, then

a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}b
.

Proof.

Suppose not. Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/b)=0
since
{\operatorname{tp}}(a)
is cgp, and so
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{b}a
.
By
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}b
and transitivity, we obtain
d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}ab
, and hence

d\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}ab
since
{\operatorname{tp}}(d)
is
\varepsilon
-wgp.
But
d={\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(ab/d)
, contradicting
d\notin{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
.
∎

A.2.
Quantitative non-expansion for actions on curves

We now give a quantitative version of Theorem
5.18
in the case
that
X
is an algebraic curve.

Fact A.11
.

[
13
, Theorem 1.3]

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{>0}
,
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}>0
and
r,k\geq 1
, there are
M\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta>0
such that the following holds.
Suppose
G
is a subgroup of
\mathrm{PGL}_{n}(K)
or
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(K)
for some field
K
of characteristic 0 and that
G
acts on some set
X
.
Suppose there are finite sets
S\subseteq G
,
T\subseteq X
with
G=\langle S\rangle
satisfying:

(1)

M<\log|T|<r\log|S|
;

(2)

|S\cap gH|<|S|^{1-\gamma}
for any nilpotent subgroup
H\leq G
of step at most
n-1
and any
g\in G
;

(3)

|\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\in T^{k}:{\operatorname{Stab}}_{G}(x_{1},x_{2},..,x_{k})\neq\mathrm{id}_{G}\}|\leq\frac{1}{M}|T|^{k-\gamma^{\prime}}
.

Then
|\{(x,y,g):x,y\in T,\;g\in S,\;x=g*y\}|\leq|T|^{1-\delta}|S|
. In fact any
\delta<\min\{\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{k},2^{-\lceil\frac{2kr}{c\gamma}\rceil-2}\}
works, where
c
is a constant depending only on
n
.

The following fact is a special case of the general model theoretic result
[
17
, Theorem 3.27]
. It can alternatively be seen by reducing to the case of a smooth projective curve and considering its automorphism group, taking cases according to the genus.

Fact A.12
.

If
X
is an algebraic curve, then any CFAHS
(G,X)
over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}
is isomorphic over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}
to one of the following:

•

(G,G)
for a connected commutative 1-dimensional algebraic group
G
acting on itself by addition.

•

({\operatorname{AGL}}_{1},{\mathbb{A}}^{1})
, the group of affine linear transformations
{\operatorname{AGL}}_{1}={\mathbb{G}}_{a}\rtimes{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
acting on the affine line.

•

({\operatorname{PSL}}_{2},\mathbb{P}^{1})
, the group of Möbius transformations acting on the projective line.

Lemma A.13
.

There is an absolute constant
c^{\prime}>0
for which the following holds.
6
6

6

This constant comes from the constants for the Balog-Szemerédi-Gowers theorem and the product theorem for
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

Let
(G,X)
be a CFAHS defined over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
with
\dim^{0}(X)=1
.

Let
a\in X(K)
and
g\in G(K)
.
Suppose
{\operatorname{tp}}(g)
is
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-wgp,
g\notin{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
,
{\operatorname{tp}}(a)
is broad,
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}g\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},\varepsilon}g*a
,
where
\varepsilon<\min\{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)2^{-\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{c^{\prime}\varepsilon^{\prime}}-5},\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)}{4},\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{4}\}
.

Then
g
is generic in a left coset over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
of a connected one-dimensional subgroup
H\leq G
.

In particular, if
g\in G(K)
is generic, then
G
is abelian of dimension 1, and so
(G,X)
is isomorphic over
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)

with the homogeneous space
(G,G)
in which
G
acts on itself by addition.

Proof.

We conclude immediately if
\dim^{0}(G)=1
. Otherwise, by Fact
A.12
,
we may assume
(G,X)=({\operatorname{AGL}}_{1},{\mathbb{A}}^{1})
or
(G,X)=({\operatorname{PSL}}_{2},\mathbb{P}^{1})
. We conclude by showing that
Y:={\operatorname{loc}}^{0}(g)
is a left coset of a connected one-dimensional subgroup of
G
. Suppose not, then either
\dim^{0}(g)\geq 2
or
\dim^{0}(g)=1
and
Y\cap hH
is finite for any one-dimensional subgroup
H\leq G
and
h\in G
.

Note that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(g*a)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(g*a/g)+\varepsilon=\boldsymbol{\delta}(a/g)+\varepsilon\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+\varepsilon
. Similarly,
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(g*a)+\varepsilon
.

Let
\Gamma_{(G,X)}=\{(x,y,g)\in X^{2}\times G:x=g*y\}
. Let
\pi_{i}:\Gamma_{(G,X)}\to X
for
i=0,1
and
\pi_{2}:\Gamma_{(G,X)}\to G
be the co-ordinate projections.
By Fact
2.4
, there is an internal set
A\subseteq{\operatorname{tp}}((g*a)ag)
such that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)=\boldsymbol{\delta}((g*a)ag)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)-\varepsilon
.
Let
T:=\pi_{0}(A)\cup\pi_{1}(A)
. Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(T)=\max\{\pi_{0}(A),\pi_{1}(A)\}\leq\max\{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a),\boldsymbol{\delta}(g*a)\}\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+\varepsilon
.
Let
S:=\pi_{2}(A)
. Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)
.

Note that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(T)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}((T^{2}\times S)\cap\Gamma_{(G,X)})\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)-\varepsilon
. Combining with
\boldsymbol{\delta}(T)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+\varepsilon
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)
, we get
\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)-2\varepsilon
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(T)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)-\varepsilon
.
Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(T)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+\varepsilon\leq\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)-2\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+\varepsilon)<\frac{3\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)}\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)
, where the last inequality is by
\varepsilon<\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)}{4}
and
\varepsilon<\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{2}
.
Also,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\delta}((T^{2}\times S)\cap\Gamma_{(G,X)})

\displaystyle\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)})+\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)\geq(\boldsymbol{\delta}(T)-\varepsilon)(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)})+\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)

\displaystyle\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(T)(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)})-\varepsilon+\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)>\boldsymbol{\delta}(T)(1-\frac{3\varepsilon}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)})+\boldsymbol{\delta}(S),

since
1<\frac{2\boldsymbol{\delta}(T)}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}
.

Suppose
T=\lim_{n\to\mathcal{U}}T_{n}
and
S=\lim_{n\to\mathcal{U}}S_{n}
.
Let
M\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Then for ultrafilter-many
n
, we have
M<\log|T_{n}|\leq 3\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)}\log|S_{n}|
and

|\{(x,y,g):x,y\in T_{n},\;g\in S_{n},\;x=g*y\}|\geq|T_{n}|^{1-\frac{3\varepsilon}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}}|S_{n}|.

Let
G_{n}=\langle S_{n}\rangle
, then
G_{n}\leq G({\mathbb{C}})
. Since
G_{n}
is a group of affine linear transformations or of elements of
\mathrm{PSL}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})
acting on
\mathbb{P}^{1}({\mathbb{C}})
, at most three distinct points determine an element in
G_{n}
. Hence,

\displaystyle|\{(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in T_{n}:{\operatorname{Stab}}_{G_{n}}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\neq\mathrm{id}\}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\{(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in T_{n}^{3}:x_{i}=x_{j}\text{ for some }i\neq j\}|\leq 3|T_{n}|^{2}.

Moreover, we claim there are ultrafilter-many
n
, such that
|S_{n}\cap hH|<|S_{n}|^{1-\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{2\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)}}
for any abelian subgroup
H\leq G_{n}
and
h\in G_{n}
. Suppose not, then for ultrafilter-many
n
, there are abelian subgroups
H_{n}\leq G_{n}
and
h_{n}\in G_{n}
such that
|S_{n}\cap h_{n}H_{n}|\geq|S_{n}|^{1-\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{2\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)}}
. Let
H:=\prod_{n\to\mathcal{U}}H_{n}
and
h:=(h_{n})_{n}/\mathcal{U}
. Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}({\operatorname{tp}}(g)\cap hH)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(S\cap hH)\geq(1-\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{2\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)})\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)-\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{2}\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)-2\varepsilon-\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{2}\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)-\varepsilon^{\prime}
, as
\varepsilon<\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{4}
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)
.
Let
\bar{H}
be the Zariski closure of
H
, then
\bar{H}
is also an abelian subgroup of
G
. Since
G={\operatorname{AGL}}_{1}
or
G=\mathrm{PSL}_{2}
, we have
1\geq\dim^{0}(\bar{H})=\dim^{0}(h\bar{H})
. If
\dim^{0}(g)\geq 2
, by
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-wgp of
{\operatorname{tp}}(g)
we have
\boldsymbol{\delta}({\operatorname{tp}}(g)\cap h\bar{H})<\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)-\varepsilon^{\prime}
, and if
\dim^{0}(g)=1
, since
Y\cap h\bar{H}
is finite we again have
\boldsymbol{\delta}({\operatorname{tp}}(g)\cap h\bar{H})=0<\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)-\varepsilon^{\prime}
(since
{\operatorname{tp}}(g)
is
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-wgp and non-algebraic, so
\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)>\varepsilon^{\prime}
). This contradicts
\boldsymbol{\delta}({\operatorname{tp}}(g)\cap hH)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)-\varepsilon^{\prime}
.

Now we may apply Fact
A.11
with
k:=3,r:=3\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)},\gamma:=\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{2\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)}
and
\gamma^{\prime}:=3/4
. We get

\min\left\{2^{-\lceil\frac{36\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{c\varepsilon^{\prime}}\rceil-2},\frac{1}{4}\right\}\leq\frac{3\varepsilon}{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)},

where
c
is the absolute constant from the bound of Fact
A.11
for

n=2
. Setting
c^{\prime}:=c/36
, then

\varepsilon\geq\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{3}2^{-\lceil\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{c^{\prime}\varepsilon^{\prime}}\rceil-2}\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)2^{-\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{c^{\prime}\varepsilon^{\prime}}-5},

contradicting the assumption on
\epsilon
. ∎

A.3.
Abelianity of cgp
(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence triangles

Recall that Proposition
7.12
asserts that cgp correspondence triangles are abelian. We now present a quantitative version of this result. We also include a special case of semi-cgp correspondence triangles, which will later be used to deduce the quantitative asymmetric Elekes–Rónyai theorem.

Proposition A.14
.

Let
(a,d,b)
be a semi-cgp
(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence triangle.

Suppose one of the following holds

(1)

d
is
\gamma
-cgp and

\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)=\dim^{0}(d)
;

(2)

d\in{\operatorname{acl}}(c_{i}:i<m)
with each
\dim^{0}(c_{i})=1
and
(c_{i})_{i<m}
is a
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}
-independent sequence such that
\max\{\boldsymbol{\delta}(c_{i}):i<m\}=1
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(c_{i})\geq r
for each
i<m
.

In case (1), set
r:=1
and
m:=\dim^{0}(d)
.
In case (2), set
\gamma:=0
.
Let
i_{0}:=1+\lfloor\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{r}\rfloor
.
Suppose
\varepsilon\leq\min\{\frac{r-2^{i_{0}}\gamma}{2^{2i_{0}+4}m},\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{2^{i_{0}+1}},\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{20}\}
.

Suppose further that
\dim^{0}(a)=1
and
\varepsilon<\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)2^{-\frac{2\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{c^{\prime}\varepsilon^{\prime}}-7}
where
c^{\prime}
is the absolute constant from Fact
A.11
.

Then
(a,d,b)
is abelian.

Before turning to the details, let us summarise the proof strategy. We follow the proof of Proposition
7.12
. Starting from
(a,d,b)
, we recursively construct
(\epsilon_{i},\epsilon_{i}’)
-correspondence triangles
(a,d_{i},b_{i})
, where the parameters
\epsilon_{i}
and
\epsilon_{i}’
are worsened exponentially with
i
. As in the proof of Proposition
7.12
, the goal is to show that
\dim^{0}(d_{i})
stabilises after finitely many steps.

To achieve this, we use the explicit Szemerédi–Trotter bound established in Lemma
A.8
, which yields an explicit bound
i_{0}
on the number of iterations required before
\dim^{0}(d_{i})
stabilises. Once this happens, we conclude that
(a,d_{i},b_{i})
is generic in the graph of a CFAHS
(G,X)
. Under the additional assumption that
\dim^{0}(a)=1
, which implies that
X
is an algebraic curve, we use Lemma
A.13
to conclude that such non-expansion cannot occur unless
G
is abelian.

Note that the exponential dependence on
i_{0}
arises because the error parameters are weakened by a squaring operation at each iteration.

Proof.

We recursively define a sequence
(d_{i},d_{i}^{\prime},b_{i})_{i\leq i_{0}}
such that,
for all
0<i\leq i_{0}
,

(i)

(a,d_{i},b_{i})
is a
(2^{i}\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}-(2^{i}-1)\varepsilon)
-correspondence
triangle,

(ii)

\dim^{0}(d_{i})\geq\dim^{0}(d_{i-1})
,

(iii)

b_{i}d_{i-1}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{b_{i-1}}ad_{i-1}
,
and
\dim^{0}(d_{i-1}^{\prime})=\dim^{0}(d_{i-1})
,
and
(b_{i},d_{i-1}^{\prime},b_{i-1})
is a

(2^{i-1}\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}-(2^{i-1}-1)\varepsilon)
-correspondence triangle,

(iv)

d_{i-1}\sim^{0}_{d_{i}}d_{i-1}^{\prime}
,

(v)

d_{i}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\bar{h})
for some finite
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}
-independent
sequence
\bar{h}
of
2^{i}
realisations of
{\operatorname{tp}}(d)
in case (1) and of
{\operatorname{tp}}((c_{i})_{i<m})
in case (2), with
\bar{h}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}b_{i}
and
\bar{h}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}a
.

First, let
(a,d_{0},b_{0}):=(a,d,b)
,
\bar{h}:=d
in case (1) and
\bar{h}:=(c_{i})_{i<m}
in case (2). Suppose
0\leq i<i_{0}
and we have defined

(d_{i},b_{i})
and
(d_{j},d_{j}^{\prime},b_{j})_{j<i}
satisfying (i)-(v).
We define
d_{i}^{\prime}
,
d_{i+1}
,
b_{i+1}
such that

(d_{i},d_{i}^{\prime},b_{i},d_{i+1},b_{i+1})
satisfy (i)-(v).

Let
\bar{h}
be as in (v) for
d_{i}
.
Let
b_{i+1}d_{i}^{\prime}\bar{h}^{\prime}\equiv_{b_{i}}ad_{i}\bar{h}
with
b_{i+1}d_{i}^{\prime}\bar{h}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{b_{i}}ad_{i}\bar{h}
,
and set
d_{i+1}:={\operatorname{cb}}^{0}(ab_{i+1}/d_{i}d_{i}^{\prime})
.
Note that
\varepsilon^{\prime}-(2^{i}-1)\varepsilon\geq 3\cdot 2^{i}\varepsilon
since
\varepsilon^{\prime}-(2^{i}-1)\varepsilon-3\cdot 2^{i}\varepsilon=\varepsilon^{\prime}-(2^{i+2}-1)\varepsilon\geq 0
.

So (i),(ii),(iv) hold for
i+1
by Lemma
A.9
, and (iii) holds by
construction. By definition,
d_{i+1}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(d_{i}d_{i}^{\prime})\subseteq{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\bar{h}\bar{h}^{\prime})
. Note that
\bar{h}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}\bar{h}^{\prime}
, since

\bar{h}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}b_{i}
by assumption. Therefore,
\bar{h}\bar{h}^{\prime}
is a

\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i+1}\varepsilon}
-independent sequence in
{\operatorname{tp}}(d)
or in
{\operatorname{tp}}((c_{i}))_{i<m})
. Moreover, we have

b_{i+1}\bar{h}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}\bar{h}
(again by
\bar{h}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}b_{i}
).
Now
\bar{h}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}b_{i+1}
since
\bar{h}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}a
,
so
\bar{h}\bar{h}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i+1}\varepsilon}b_{i+1}
.
Also,
\bar{h}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}b_{i}
since
\bar{h}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}b_{i}
, so
\bar{h}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}\bar{h}a
, and since also
\bar{h}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}a
, we have
\bar{h}\bar{h}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i+1}\varepsilon}a
.
So (v) holds, witnessed by
\bar{h}\bar{h}^{\prime}
.

This concludes our construction of the sequence

(d_{i},d_{i}^{\prime},b_{i})_{i\in\omega}
.

Now for all
i<i_{0}
, we have:

a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}d_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}b_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}a
and

b_{i+1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}d^{\prime}_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}b_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}b_{i+1}
by (i) (which also holds for

i=0
), (iii), Remark
A.6
, and Lemma
A.10
;

•

d_{i}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}b_{i}d_{i}
, and hence
b_{i+1}d_{i}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}_{b_{i}}ad_{i}
:
indeed,
d_{i}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}b_{i}d_{i}
and
d_{i}^{\prime}
is

(\varepsilon^{\prime}-(2^{i}-1)\varepsilon)
-wgp and
\varepsilon^{\prime}-(2^{i}-1)\varepsilon>2^{i}\varepsilon
, so
d_{i}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}b_{i}d_{i}
.

Claim A.15
.

For all
0<i\leq i_{0}
,

\dim^{0}(d_{i-1})\leq\dim^{0}(d_{i})\leq\frac{1}{r}(\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+2^{2i+4}m\varepsilon+2^{i}\gamma).

Proof.

The first inequality is by (ii).

Let
r=1
in case (1) and
\gamma=0
in case (2). Note that in both cases, we have
\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)\geq r\dim^{0}(d)
(by Lemma
A.4
in case (2)) and
m\geq\dim^{0}(d)
.
By Lemma
A.8
, we have

\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)-(6\dim^{0}(d)+5)\varepsilon\geq r\dim^{0}(d)-(6m+5)\varepsilon\geq\frac{r}{2}>3\cdot 2^{i_{0}}\varepsilon\geq 3\cdot 2^{i}\varepsilon,

since
(6m+5)\varepsilon\leq\frac{r}{2}
and
\varepsilon<\frac{r}{6\cdot 2^{i_{0}}}
,
so by (i) and Lemma
A.8
again, we obtain

(5)

\boldsymbol{\delta}(d_{i})\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+2^{i}\varepsilon(6\dim^{0}(d_{i})+5).

In case (1), by (v),
d_{i}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\bar{h})
for some
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}
-independent sequence

\bar{h}

of
2^{i}
realisations of
{\operatorname{tp}}(d)
.
By Lemma
A.4
(and
since we assumed
\dim^{0}(d)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)
), we obtain

\dim^{0}(d_{i})\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(d_{i})+2^{2i}\varepsilon+2^{i}\gamma
.

In case (2), since
d_{i}\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\bar{h})
for some
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}
-independent sequence

\bar{h}

of
2^{i}
realisations of
{\operatorname{tp}}((c_{i})_{i<m})
and
(c_{i})_{i<m}
is a
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}
-independence sequence, we have
\bar{h}

is a

\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta},2^{i}\varepsilon}
-independent sequence of
m\cdot 2^{i}
realisations of the types among
\{{\operatorname{tp}}(c_{i}):{i<m}\}
.
So by Lemma
A.4
, we obtain

r\dim^{0}(d_{i})\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(d_{i})+2^{2i}m\varepsilon.

In both cases,

\dim^{0}(d_{i})\leq\frac{1}{r}(\boldsymbol{\delta}(d_{i})+2^{2i}m\varepsilon+2^{i}\gamma).

Combining with the inequality (
5
), we conclude

\dim^{0}(d_{i})\leq\frac{1}{r}(\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+(2^{i}(6\dim^{0}(d_{i})+5)+2^{2i}m)\varepsilon+2^{i}\gamma).

Using the coarse bounds
\dim^{0}(d_{i})\leq 2^{i}\dim^{0}(d)\leq 2^{i}m
, this gives

\dim^{0}(d_{i})\leq\frac{1}{r}(\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+2^{2i+4}m\varepsilon+2^{i}\gamma)
.
∎
A.15

Suppose for a contradiction that

\dim^{0}(d_{i+1})>\dim^{0}(d_{i})
for all
i<i_{0}
.
Then

\dim^{0}(d)+i_{0}\leq\dim^{0}(d_{i_{0}})\leq\frac{1}{r}(\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+2^{2i_{0}+4}m\varepsilon+2^{i_{0}}\gamma).

By our assumptions,
\varepsilon\leq\frac{r-2^{i_{0}}\gamma}{2^{2i_{0}+4}m}
, so

\dim^{0}(d)+i_{0}\leq\frac{1}{r}(\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+2^{2i_{0}+4}m\varepsilon+2^{i_{0}}\gamma)\leq\frac{1}{r}(\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)+r-2^{i_{0}}\gamma+2^{i_{0}}\gamma)=\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{r}+1.

Thus
i_{0}\leq\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{r}
(as
\dim^{0}(d)\geq 1
by the definition of
(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence triangle),
contradicting

i_{0}\geq\lfloor\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{r}\rfloor+1
.

So
\dim^{0}(d_{i+1})=\dim^{0}(d_{i})
for some
i<i_{0}
.

As in the proof of Proposition
7.12
,
Proposition
6.4
yields a CFAHS
(G,X)
over

{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)
such that up to
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}
-interalgebraicity,

(d_{i},a,b_{i})
is generic in the graph of the action.

Now, assume
\dim^{0}(a)=1
, then
\dim(X)=1
and
\dim^{0}(d_{i})\leq 3
, since
\dim(G)
is bounded by 3 by Fact
A.12
. Hence, already
\dim^{0}(d_{i+1})=\dim^{0}(d_{i})
for
i\leq 2
. Now, we have a
(4\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}-3\varepsilon)
-correspondence triangle
(a,d_{2},b_{2})
which is, up to
{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}
-interalgebraicity,
generic in the graph of the action of
G
on
X
.

By Lemma
A.13
,
G
is commutative provided

4\varepsilon<\min\{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)2^{-\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{c^{\prime}(\varepsilon^{\prime}-3\varepsilon)}-5},\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}-3\varepsilon}{4},\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(d_{2})}{4}\},

which holds when
\varepsilon<\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)2^{-\frac{2\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{c^{\prime}\varepsilon^{\prime}}-7}
(since
\varepsilon^{\prime}-3\varepsilon>\varepsilon^{\prime}/2
) and
\varepsilon<\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{20}
(note that
4\varepsilon<r/4\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(d)/4\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(d_{2})/4
). And we then conclude exactly
as in the proof of Proposition
7.12
.
∎

Remark A.16
.

Proposition
A.14
is our quantitative analog of Proposition
7.12
for cgp triangles.
We could hope the same method would yield a quantitative version of
Theorem
7.20
for the semi-cgp triangles, by perturbing the assumptions and trying to control the error occurring in the proof. Attempting to do this leads however to the problem that the
parameter
c
in the proof of Proposition
7.12
, chosen such that

{\operatorname{tp}}(d/c)
is cgp, has unbounded transcendence degree
\dim^{0}(c)
. If we
assume that the semi-cgp triangle
(a,d,b)
is an

(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-correspondence triangle, we obtain
\boldsymbol{\delta}(c)\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}
, but
in order to later obtain
c\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{0}c^{\prime}
we need to be able to add parameters

f
such that
c
becomes
\varepsilon
-wgp. However, ensuring this appears to
require assuming
\varepsilon^{\prime}\geq\dim^{0}(c)\varepsilon
. Since the geometry of the
situation gives no upper bound on
\dim^{0}(c)
, this prevents us from giving
a direct bound on the powersaving as in Proposition
A.14
. For
this reason, we content ourselves with qualitative statements in this
regime.

A.4.
The case of a polynomial

Here, we deduce our quantative analogue of Lemma
9.2
, used in the explicit Elekes-Rónyai result Theorem
9.8
.

Lemma A.17
.

Let
f\in K[t]
, and suppose that for some
a\in K
, setting
b:=f(a)
and identifying
f
with its tuple of coefficients, there exist
r
,
\eta
, and
m
such that:

(1)

(a,f,b)
is a semi-cgp
(\eta,r)
-correspondence triangle;

(2)

0<r\leq 1
;

(3)

\eta<\min\{\frac{r}{2^{2i_{0}+4}m},\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)2^{-\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{cr}-7}\}
, where
i_{0}:=1+\lfloor\frac{\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)}{r}\rfloor
and
c
is an absolute constant;

(4)

f\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}((c_{i})_{i<m})
for some
\boldsymbol{\delta}
-independent sequence
(c_{i})_{i<m}
such that
\dim^{0}(c_{i})=1
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(c_{i})\geq r
for all
i<m
, and
\max\{\boldsymbol{\delta}(c_{i}):i<m\}=1
.

Then there are
h_{0},g\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(\emptyset)[t]
and
d\in{\operatorname{acl}}^{0}(f)
such that

f(x)=h_{0}(g(x)+_{G}d)
, where
+_{G}
is either addition or multiplication.

Proof.

The proof is identical to that of Lemma
9.2
, with the following two exceptions:

•

We apply Proposition
A.14
rather than Theorem
7.20
at the start of the proof;

•

We apply Lemma
A.13
rather than Corollary
5.22
near the end, noting that
(a^{\prime},\phi_{f}^{\prime},b^{\prime})
remains a semi-cgp
(\eta,r)
-correspondence triangle satisfying the conditions of Lemma
A.13
for
{\operatorname{AGL}}_{1}={\mathbb{G}}_{a}\rtimes{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
acting on
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
.∎

Appendix B
Balog-Szemerédi-Gowers for group actions

In this section, we show how our techniques can be applied to obtain a Balog-Szemerédi-Gowers type theorem for arbitrary group actions
(G,X)
. Because of the existence of stabilisers, it is not true any more that from non-expansion (i.e.,
\boldsymbol{\delta}(S*A)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
for some
S\subseteq G
and
A\subseteq X
of positive
\boldsymbol{\delta}
) one can always find approximate subgroups. It is true once we impose some conditions on stabilisers. Similar results were previously obtained in
[
15
]
.

First, we prove a version for types. As discussed in §
5
, this generalises a part of the proof of Theorem
5.18
. As there, we define an increasing sequence of group elements with
\boldsymbol{\delta}
which is bounded above and hence tends to a limit. For this argument to go through, we impose a technical condition ((3) below) on the
\boldsymbol{\delta}
of the stabilisers.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
M^{\mathcal{L}}
be an adequate expansion of
M=(G,X)
.
Suppose there are constants
t\geq 0,n\in\mathbb{N}
, and elements
g\in G
and
a\in X
, such that, setting
\lambda:=\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)
and
\mu:=\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)
, we have:

(1)

0<\lambda,\mu<\infty
;

(2)

a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}g\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}g*a
;

(3)

There exists
\bar{a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
with
a_{1}=a
, and
a_{i}\equiv_{g}a
, and
a_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{g}(a_{j})_{j<i}
, such that for any
\boldsymbol{\delta}
-independent sequence
(g_{i})_{i\in I}
in
{\operatorname{tp}}(g)
and any
h\in\langle g_{i}:i\in I\rangle
,
we have
\boldsymbol{\delta}(h/\bar{a},h*\bar{a})\leq t\lambda.

Then there are
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}\supseteq{\mathcal{L}}
and an adequate expansion
M^{{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}
,
and a complete
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
-type
p
in the sort
G
with
\boldsymbol{\delta}(p)\in[\mu,(n+t)\lambda]
,
and a complete
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
-type
q\supseteq{\operatorname{tp}}^{{\mathcal{L}}}(a)
in the sort
X
with
\boldsymbol{\delta}(q)=\lambda
, such that the following hold.

(I)

There exists
h\in G
such that
\boldsymbol{\delta}((h\cdot p(G))\cap{\operatorname{tp}}^{{\mathcal{L}}}(g))\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}({\operatorname{tp}}^{\mathcal{L}}(g))
.

(II)

For all
m
, for all
s_{i}\vDash p
, for all
\varepsilon_{i}\in\{-1,1\}
and for all
a^{\prime}\vDash q
:

•

\boldsymbol{\delta}(\prod_{i\leq m}s_{i}^{\varepsilon_{i}})\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(p)
;

•

\boldsymbol{\delta}((\prod_{i\leq m}s_{i}^{\varepsilon_{i}})*a^{\prime})\leq\lambda
.

Proof.

By Claim
5.20
in the proof of Theorem
5.18
, we can define
(g_{i},g_{i}^{\prime})_{i\in\omega}
and
\delta
-independent sequences
(\bar{h}_{i})_{i\in\omega}
and
(\bar{h}^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in\omega}
in
{\operatorname{tp}}(g)
such that
g_{i+1}=g_{i}^{\prime-1}\cdot g_{i}
,
g_{i}\in\langle\bar{h}_{i}\rangle
,
g_{i}\bar{h}_{i}\equiv g_{i}^{\prime}\bar{h}_{i}^{\prime}
,
\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i}/g_{i}\cdot g^{-1})=\mu
and

{\bar{a}}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}g_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}g_{i}*{\bar{a}},\quad g_{i}^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}g_{i}{\bar{a}}.

We claim that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i})\leq(n+t)\lambda
.
Indeed,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i},\bar{a})-\boldsymbol{\delta}(\bar{a}/g_{i})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i},\bar{a},g_{i}*\bar{a})-\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i}*\bar{a}/g_{i})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\delta}(\bar{a},g_{i}*{\bar{a}})+\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i}/\bar{a},g_{i}*{\bar{a}})-\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i}*\bar{a})\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}({\bar{a}})+\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i}/\bar{a},g_{i}*\bar{a}).

Since
g_{i}\in\langle\bar{h}_{i}\rangle
and
\bar{h}_{i}
is a
\boldsymbol{\delta}
-independent sequence in
{\operatorname{tp}}(g)

\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i}/\bar{a},g_{i}*\bar{a})\leq t\lambda
.
Thus,
\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i})\leq(n+t)\lambda
.
Moreover,
\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i+1})=\boldsymbol{\delta}((g_{i}^{\prime})^{-1}\cdot g_{i})\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i}^{\prime}/g_{i})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i})
.
In conclusion, the sequence
(\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i}))_{i\in\omega}
is non-decreasing and bounded, hence tends to some value
\nu\in[\mu,(n+t)\lambda]
.

Let
(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},a^{\prime},g^{\prime})
and an adequate expansion

(
c

,
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a^{\prime}/\gamma)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(a^{\prime})=\lambda=\boldsymbol{\delta}(\gamma*a^{\prime})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(\gamma*a^{\prime}/\gamma)
(since
\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\mathcal{L}}(g_{i}*a)=\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\mathcal{L}}(a)=\lambda
and

for all

In conclusion, we have

\gamma\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\gamma^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern .
Suppose

\boldsymbol{\delta}((h\cdot p(G))\cap S^{\prime})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(p(G)\cap S^{\prime}_{h})\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(\beta^{-1}\cdot\gamma/h)\geq\mu,

For a subset
S\subseteq G
and
a\in X^{n}
, we write
{\operatorname{Stab}}_{S}(a)
for
{\operatorname{Stab}}_{G}(a)\cap S
.

Theorem B.2
.

Suppose there are
n\in\mathbb{N}^{>0}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}^{>0}
and
internal sets
S\subseteq G
with
S=S^{-1}
and
A,B\subseteq X
, satisfying:

(1)

0<\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(B)<\infty
,
0<\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)<\infty
;

(2)

\boldsymbol{\delta}(\{(a^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,a^{\prime}_{n})\in A^{n}:\boldsymbol{\delta}(\mathrm{Stab}_{S^{k}}(a^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,a^{\prime}_{n}))\geq t\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)\})<n\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
for all
k
;
7
7

7

By continuity of
\boldsymbol{\delta}
, the set
\{(a^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,a^{\prime}_{n})\in A^{n}:\boldsymbol{\delta}(\mbox{Stab}_{S^{k}}(a^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,a_{n}^{\prime})\geq t\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)\}
is
\bigwedge
-definable over
\emptyset
, hence it makes sense to talk about its
\boldsymbol{\delta}
-value.

(3)

(Large-incidence)
\boldsymbol{\delta}(\{(s,a,b)\in S\times A\times B:s*a=b\})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
.

Then there exist an internal coarse approximate subgroup
H\leq G
and an internal subset
T\subseteq A
such that the following holds:

(I)

\boldsymbol{\delta}(T)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
, and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(H)\leq(n+t)\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
, and there is
h\in G
with
\boldsymbol{\delta}(h\cdot H\cap S)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)
for some
h\in G
;

(II)

(Non-expansion)
\boldsymbol{\delta}(H*T)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(T)
.

Proof.

Work in an adequate expansion of
(G,X,S,A,B)
.
Let
(g,a,g*a)
be an element of the definable set
\{(g,a,b)\in S\times A\times B:g*a=b\}
such that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(g,a,g*a)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(g,a)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(g,g*a)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)+\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
. Then,
g\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}a
and
g\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}g*a
,
and
0<\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)<\infty
by assumption.
Let
a_{1}=a
and for
1\leq i\leq n
, inductively define
a_{i}\equiv_{g}a
with
a_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{g}(a_{j})_{j<i}
.
Let
\bar{a}:=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
.
Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(\bar{a})=n\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
.

To apply Lemma
B.1
, we need to show that

\boldsymbol{\delta}(h/\bar{a},h*\bar{a})\leq t\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)

for any
h\in\langle\bar{g}\rangle
where
\bar{g}
is a
\boldsymbol{\delta}
-independent sequence in
{\operatorname{tp}}(g)
. Since each element in
\bar{g}
is in
S
, there is some
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
h\in S^{m}
.
Let
D:=\{h^{\prime}\in S^{m}:h^{\prime}*\bar{a}=h*\bar{a}\}
, then
{\operatorname{tp}}(h/\bar{a},h*\bar{a})\vDash D
.
We only need to show that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)\leq t\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)
.
Note that
h^{-1}\cdot D\subseteq\mathrm{Stab}_{S^{2m}}(\bar{a})
.
If
\boldsymbol{\delta}(D)>t\boldsymbol{\delta}(a)
, then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(\mbox{Stab}_{S^{2m}}(\bar{a}))\geq t\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
.
By continuity of
\boldsymbol{\delta}
, the set

D_{t}:=\{(a^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,a^{\prime}_{n})\in A^{n}:\boldsymbol{\delta}(\mbox{Stab}_{S^{2m}}(a^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,a_{n}^{\prime})\geq t\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)\}

is
\bigwedge
-definable over
\emptyset
. Therefore, it contains all realisations of
{\operatorname{tp}}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
.
Thus
\boldsymbol{\delta}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(D_{t})<n\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
, a contradiction.

Let
h\in G
and complete types
p
and
q
in an adequate expansion
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
be as given by Lemma
B.1
. Then together with Lemma
2.5
, we have

•

\boldsymbol{\delta}(p)\leq(n+t)\boldsymbol{\delta}({\operatorname{tp}}^{\mathcal{L}}(a))=(n+t)\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
,
\boldsymbol{\delta}(q)=\boldsymbol{\delta}({\operatorname{tp}}^{\mathcal{L}}(a))=\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
;

•

\boldsymbol{\delta}((h\cdot p(G))\cap S)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}({\operatorname{tp}}^{\mathcal{L}}(g))=\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)
;

•

\boldsymbol{\delta}(p(G)\cdot p(G)\cdot p(G))=\boldsymbol{\delta}(p(G))
;

•

\boldsymbol{\delta}((p(G)\cdot p(G)\cdot p(G))*q(X))\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}({\operatorname{tp}}^{\mathcal{L}}(a))=\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
.

Let
E\subseteq p(G)
and
T\subseteq q(X)
such that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(E)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(p)\leq(n+t)\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(T)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(q)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)
(which exist by Fact
2.4
), then

\boldsymbol{\delta}((h\cdot E)\cap S)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(S),\boldsymbol{\delta}(E\cdot E\cdot E)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(E)\text{ and }\boldsymbol{\delta}((E\cdot E\cdot E)*T)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(T).

Therefore, let
H^{\prime}:=E\cup\{1\}\cup E^{-1}
and
H:=H^{\prime}\cdot H^{\prime}\cdot H^{\prime}
, then
H
is a coarse approximate subgroup (by Fact
3.11
) such that
\boldsymbol{\delta}(H)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(p)
,
\boldsymbol{\delta}(h\cdot H\cap S)\geq\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}((H*T)\leq\boldsymbol{\delta}(T)
(since
E\subseteq H
) as desired.
∎

Remark B.3
.

In Theorem
B.2
, we can not take
H
to be a translate of a subset of any finite product
S\cdot\ldots\cdot S
, and correspondingly, in Lemma
B.1
, the limiting process is necessary and we can not take
g
to be any
g_{i}
. The following example in the case of
(K,+)
acting on itself by addition demonstrates this; in the notation of the example, if
g\in S
with
\boldsymbol{\delta}(g)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)=1
, then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i})=\boldsymbol{\delta}(\sum_{i<2^{i}}S)
so
\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i})<\boldsymbol{\delta}(g_{i+1})
.

Example B.4
.

Let
(a_{i})_{i\in\omega}
be a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linearly independent sequence of complex numbers.
For finite
N
, define:

•

n_{N}:=\lfloor\log_{2}\log_{2}N\rfloor
.

•

I_{N,i}:=([-N^{2^{-i}},N^{2^{-i}}]\cap{\mathbb{Z}})\cdot a_{i}
for
i<n_{N}
.

•

S_{N}:=\bigcup_{i<n_{N}}I_{N,i}
.

•

A_{N}:=\sum_{i<n_{N}}I_{N,i}
.

Let
(S,A):=\prod_{N\to{\mathcal{U}}}(S_{N},A_{N})
be the ultraproduct, and let
\xi:=(N)_{N\in\omega}/{\mathcal{U}}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{U}}
.
Then
\boldsymbol{\delta}(S)=1
and
\boldsymbol{\delta}(A)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(S+A)=\boldsymbol{\delta}(A+A)=2
,
but
\boldsymbol{\delta}(\sum_{i<k}S)=2-2^{-{k-1}}
.

One readily verifies the following finitary version of Theorem
B.2
.

Corollary B.5
.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{>0}
and
r,t>0
, for all
\delta>0
there is
\varepsilon>0
such that the following holds.
Let
G
be a group acting on a set
X
.
Suppose there are finite sets
S\subseteq G
and
A\subseteq X
satisfying:

(1)

\frac{1}{\varepsilon}<|A|^{\frac{1}{r}}\leq|S|\leq|A|^{r}
;

(2)

|\{(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\in A^{n}:|\mathrm{Stab}_{\langle S\rangle}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})|\geq|A|^{t}\}|\leq|A|^{n-\delta}
, where
\langle S\rangle
is the group generated by
S
;

(3)

(Large-incidence)
|\{(s,a,b)\in S\times A\times A,s*a=b\}|\geq|S||A|^{1-\varepsilon}
.

Then there exist a finite
|A|^{\delta}
-approximate subgroup
H\leq G
and a subset
T\subseteq A
such that the following holds:

(1)

|H|\leq|A|^{n+t+\delta}
,
|H\cap h\cdot S|\geq|A|^{-\delta}|S|
for some
h\in G
;

(2)

|T|\geq|A|^{1-\delta}
;

(3)

(Non-expansion)
|H*T|\leq|A|^{1+\delta}
.
